This article is about the musician. For other uses, see [Madonna (disambiguation)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madonna_\(disambiguation\)).

**Madonna Louise Ciccone**[^cite_note-2] ([/tʃɪˈkoʊni/](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help\:IPA/English) [*chih-KOH-nee*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help\:Pronunciation_respelling_key), Italian: [\[tʃikˈkoːne\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help\:IPA/Italian); born August 16, 1958) is an American singer, songwriter, record producer, and actress. Dubbed the "**Queen of Pop**", she is known for her continual reinvention and versatility in music production, songwriting, and visual presentation. Her works, which concern social, political, [sexual](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madonna_and_sexuality), and [religious](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madonna_and_religion) themes, have drawn acclaim and controversy. Regarded as one of the most influential musicians in history, Madonna broke [gender barriers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gender_inequality) in [popular music](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Popular_music) and has had [a significant socio-cultural impact](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cultural_impact_of_Madonna) across her career.&#x20;

Born and raised in [Michigan](18859.md), Madonna moved to New York City in 1978 to pursue a career in dance. After performing as a drummer, guitarist, and vocalist in the rock bands [Breakfast Club](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Breakfast_Club_\(band\)) and Emmy, she rose to fame with her debut album, *[Madonna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madonna_\(album\))* (1983). Her eighteen multi-platinum albums include *[Like a Virgin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Like_a_Virgin_\(album\))* (1984), *[True Blue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/True_Blue_\(album\))* (1986), and *[The Immaculate Collection](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Immaculate_Collection)* (1990)—among the [best-selling albums of all time](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_best-selling_albums)—and *[Confessions on a Dance Floor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Confessions_on_a_Dance_Floor)* (2005), her [21st-century bestseller](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_best-selling_albums_of_the_21st_century). *[Rolling Stone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rolling_Stone)* ranked *[Like a Prayer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Like_a_Prayer_\(album\))* (1989), *[Ray of Light](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ray_of_Light)* (1998), and *[Music](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Music_\(Madonna_album\))* (2000) among the [greatest albums of all time](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rolling_Stone's_500_Greatest_Albums_of_All_Time). Madonna's top-charting [singles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madonna_singles_discography) include "[Like a Virgin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Like_a_Virgin_\(song\))", "[Papa Don't Preach](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Papa_Don't_Preach)", "[La Isla Bonita](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/La_Isla_Bonita)", "[Like a Prayer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Like_a_Prayer_\(song\))", "[Vogue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vogue_\(song\))", "[Take a Bow](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Take_a_Bow_\(Madonna_song\))", "[Frozen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Frozen_\(Madonna_song\))", "[Music](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Music_\(Madonna_song\))", "[Hung Up](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hung_Up)", and "[4 Minutes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/4_Minutes)".

[Madonna has starred in films](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madonna_filmography) such as *[Desperately Seeking Susan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Desperately_Seeking_Susan)* (1985), *[Dick Tracy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dick_Tracy_\(1990_film\))* (1990), *[A League of Their Own](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/A_League_of_Their_Own)* (1992), and *[Evita](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Evita_\(1996_film\))* (1996). Although many of her films were not well-received, she won the [Golden Globe Award for Best Actress](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Golden_Globe_Award_for_Best_Actress_%E2%80%93_Motion_Picture_Comedy_or_Musical) for portraying [Eva Perón](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eva_Per%C3%B3n) in *Evita*. [Her business endeavors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madonna_and_business) encompass the entertainment company [Maverick](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maverick_\(company\)) (1992–2009), which included Maverick Records—one of the most successful [artist-run labels](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vanity_label). Madonna has also pursued [fashion brands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madonna_fashion_brands), [written works](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madonna_bibliography), [health clubs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hard_Candy_Fitness), and filmmaking. She contributes to [various charities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philanthropy_and_activism_of_Madonna), having founded the [Ray of Light Foundation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ray_of_Light_Foundation) in 1998 and [Raising Malawi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Raising_Malawi) in 2006, and advocates for [gender equality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Feminism_of_Madonna) and [LGBTQ rights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madonna_as_a_gay_icon).

Madonna is the [best-selling female music artist of all time](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_best-selling_music_artists), and the [first female performer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_highest-grossing_live_music_artists) to accumulate US\$1 billion from [her concerts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Madonna_concerts). She has [twelve number-one singles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Artists_with_the_most_number-ones_on_the_Billboard_Hot_100_chart) on the US [*Billboard* Hot 100](423161.md) and [ten number-one albums](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Billboard_200#Most_number-one_albums) on the US [*Billboard* 200](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Billboard_200). [Her accolades](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_awards_and_nominations_received_by_Madonna) include seven [Grammy Awards](44635.md), two [Golden Globe Awards](142724.md), 27 [MTV Video Music Awards](295217.md), and [an induction](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Rock_and_Roll_Hall_of_Fame_inductees) into the [Rock and Roll Hall of Fame](58902.md) in her first year of eligibility. Madonna was the world's [highest-paid female musician](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forbes_list_of_the_world's_highest-paid_musicians#Female) for a record eleven years across four decades (1980s–2010s). She has become the subject of various [scholarly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_academic_publishing_works_on_Madonna), [literary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bibliography_of_works_on_Madonna), and [artistic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madonna_and_contemporary_arts) works, giving rise to an academic subfield known as [Madonna studies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madonna_studies).

## Life and career

### 1958–1978: Early life

Madonna Louise Ciccone was born in [Bay City, Michigan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bay_City,_Michigan), on August 16, 1958.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETaraborrelli200211–13Bego200012Gnojewski200722-3] Her father, Silvio "Tony" Ciccone, worked as an [optical](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Optical_engineering) and [military](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Military_engineering) engineer, as well as a physicist, for [Chrysler Defense](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chrysler_Defense) and later [General Dynamics Land Systems](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_Dynamics_Land_Systems); her mother, Madonna Louise ([née](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/N%C3%A9e) Fortin), was an [X-ray technician](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/X-ray_technician).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBego200012Taraborrelli20028,_336Cross20071–3Gnojewski200725-4] Tony's parents were Italian emigrants from [Pacentro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pacentro), while her mother was of [French-Canadian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French-Canadian) descent.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETaraborrelli200210Bego200012-5] Since Madonna shared her name with her mother, family members referred to her as "Little Nonnie".[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECross20072Taraborrelli200211-6] Madonna was raised in the Detroit suburbs of [Pontiac](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pontiac,_Michigan) and Avon Township (now [Rochester Hills](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rochester_Hills)), alongside her two older brothers, Anthony and Martin, and her three younger siblings, Paula, [Christopher](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christopher_Ciccone), and Melanie.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBego200012–13Gnojewski200718-7][^cite_note-t627-8]

When Madonna was five years old, her mother died of [breast cancer](70547.md) on December 1, 1963.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHavranek2008257Bego200020-9] In 1966, she adopted Veronica as a [confirmation name](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Confirmation_name) upon receiving the [sacrament](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Confirmation_in_the_Catholic_Church) in the Catholic Church.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGuilbert200292Gnojewski2007122-10] That same year, Tony married the family's housekeeper, Joan Gustafson. They had three children: Joey (who died shortly after his birth in 1967 from a [heart defect](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heart_defect)), Jennifer, and Mario.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGnojewski200718-11][^cite_note-b454-12] Madonna attended St. Frederick's and St. Andrew's Catholic elementary schools, as well as West Middle School.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBego200018Pohlen2014273-13] She earned high grades—her father gave her a [quarter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quarter_\(United_States_coin\)) for every [A](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grading_in_education)—and was notorious for her unconventional behavior. Madonna performed cartwheels and handstands in the hallways between classes, hung upside down from the monkey bars during recess, and lifted her skirt in class to amuse the boys.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETaraborrelli200221–23Bego200018-14]

In retrospect, Madonna described herself as a "lonely girl who was searching for something", explaining: "I wasn't rebellious in a certain way. I cared about being good at something. I didn't shave under my arms and I didn't wear makeup like normal girls do. But I studied and I got good grades \[...] I wanted to be somebody."[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETaraborrelli200214-15] Her father initially enrolled her in [classical piano](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Classical_piano) lessons, but she eventually persuaded him to let her study [ballet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ballet) instead. Her ballet teacher, Christopher Flynn, inspired her to pursue a career in dance, remarking that she was the best student in the class.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECross200710–12Taraborrelli200226–29-16] While attending [Rochester Adams High School](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rochester_Adams_High_School), Madonna was a straight-A student and a member of the cheerleading squad.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEClaro199424,_27Cross20079-17] After graduating in January 1976, she received a dance scholarship to the [University of Michigan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Michigan) and spent the summer studying at the [American Dance Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Dance_Festival) in [Durham, North Carolina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Durham,_North_Carolina).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGnojewski200729–30,_32ParishPitts2003523-18]

In 1978, Madonna left college and moved to New York City.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBego200078Taraborrelli200234-19] She called the decision "the bravest thing \[she'd] ever done"; it was the first time she had ever flown or taken a taxi, and she arrived with "\$35 in \[her] pocket".[^cite_note-r547-20] She settled in the [Alphabet City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alphabet_City) area of the [East Village](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_Village,_Manhattan) and supported herself with limited means by working various jobs—including as a hatcheck girl at the [Russian Tea Room](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_Tea_Room), an elevator operator at [Terrace on the Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Terrace_on_the_Park), and a member of dance troupes.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETaraborrelli200237O'Brien200762,_68-21][^cite_note-t758-22] Madonna took classes at the [Alvin Ailey American Dance Theater](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alvin_Ailey_American_Dance_Theater), performed with the [Pearl Lang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pearl_Lang) Dance Theater, and studied under dancer and choreographer [Martha Graham](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martha_Graham).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEO'Brien200762Fuhrer2014133Taraborrelli200237–39Freedman1998125-23] One night, as she was returning from a rehearsal, two men held her at knifepoint and forced her to perform [fellatio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fellatio). She said later the incident was "a taste of my weakness, it showed me that I still could not save myself in spite of all the strong-girl show. I could never forget it."[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESteinBartone202062-24] She was also raped at knifepoint after accepting an offer to use someone's phone in their apartment in New York.[^cite_note-25]

### 1979–1983: Career beginnings, rock bands, and *Madonna*

In 1979, Madonna entered a romantic relationship with musician [Dan Gilroy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dan_Gilroy_\(musician\)).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETaraborrelli200245O'Brien200772-26] During this period, she searched for job opportunities in publications such as *[Variety](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Variety_\(magazine\))*, *[Backstage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Backstage_\(magazine\))*, and *[Show Business](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Show_Business_\(magazine\))*, leading to a successful audition as a backup singer and dancer in Paris for French disco artist [Patrick Hernandez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Patrick_Hernandez).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETaraborrelli200246–48Bego200051-27] With Hernandez's troupe, she traveled to Tunisia and several disco-oriented European countries before returning to New York that same year.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBego200052-28] Madonna relocated to an abandoned [synagogue](48559.md) in [Corona, Queens](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Corona,_Queens), where Gilroy and his brother Ed resided and practiced.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGnojewski200756Bego200050Taraborrelli200245-29] The group slept in the basement and used its meeting space both to rehearse and record music for their band, [Breakfast Club](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Breakfast_Club_\(band\)), for which Madonna sang and played drums and guitar.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBego200050Gnojewski200735-30]

Madonna made her acting debut in the low-budget indie film *[A Certain Sacrifice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/A_Certain_Sacrifice)*, which was shot in two parts between 1979 and 1981. She unsuccessfully attempted to block its 1985 release through legal action against director Stephen Jon Lewicki. The film was primarily criticized for its explicit sexual and violent content.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETaraborrelli200250–51,_103–104Koopmans200331-31] In 1980, after leaving the Breakfast Club and ending her relationship with Gilroy, Madonna reunited with drummer [Stephen Bray](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stephen_Bray), whom she had previously dated in Michigan, and together they formed the band Emmy.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETaraborrelli200253–54O'Brien200776-32] She and Bray lived and rehearsed at [the Music Building](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Music_Building) in Manhattan, where they wrote songs and recorded a four-track demo tape.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETaraborrelli200245O'Brien200776Gnojewski200760–63-33] After Madonna left Emmy, Camille Barbone, who ran [Gotham Records](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gotham_Records) in the Music Building, signed her to a contract with the label in March 1981, working as her manager until February 1982.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMorton200194,_105O'Brien200777-34] Her creative partnership with Bray continued for many years.[^cite_note-35]

In 1982, Madonna visited nightclubs to persuade DJs to play her demo, leading [Mark Kamins](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mark_Kamins) at [Danceteria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Danceteria) to take an interest in her music and begin a romance with her.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECross200725O'Brien200795–96Bego2000102-36] He arranged a meeting with [Seymour Stein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seymour_Stein), president of [Sire Records](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sire_Records)—a subsidiary of [Warner Bros. Records](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Warner_Bros._Records)—after which she signed a two-single deal.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECross200725Morton2001107-37] Kamins produced her debut single, "[Everybody](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Everybody_\(Madonna_song\))", which was released in October 1982 and promoted with television and nightclub performances.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECross200725Taraborrelli200273Morton2001118-38][^cite_note-39] Her second single, the [double A-side](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/A-side_and_B-side) "[Burning Up](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Burning_Up_\(Madonna_song\))" / "[Physical Attraction](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Physical_Attraction_\(song\))", was released in March 1983. Both "Everybody" and "Burning Up" / "Physical Attraction" reached number three on the [*Billboard* Hot Dance Club Songs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hot_Dance_Club_Songs) chart.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBego200080-40] During this period, Madonna was in a relationship with artist [Jean-Michel Basquiat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jean-Michel_Basquiat) and living in his [SoHo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SoHo) loft.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFretz2010105–106-41] Basquiat introduced her to art curator [Diego Cortez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Diego_Cortez), who had managed several [punk bands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Punk_band). Cortez declined to manage Madonna when she requested that he do so.[^cite_note-z574-42]

Warner hired [Reggie Lucas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reggie_Lucas) to produce her debut studio album, *[Madonna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madonna_\(album\))*.[^cite_note-x447-43] Madonna was dissatisfied with many of the tracks, prompting her to seek additional support. She enlisted DJ [John "Jellybean" Benitez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_%22Jellybean%22_Benitez) to help complete the album, and the two began a brief romantic relationship.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBego200080Taraborrelli200243,_82-44] Benitez remixed most of the songs and produced "[Holiday](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Holiday_\(Madonna_song\))", her breakthrough song.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHavranek2008259-45] *Madonna* was released on July 27, 1983, to generally favorable reviews, and peaked at number eight on the US [*Billboard* 200](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Billboard_200).[^cite_note-Debut30-46][^cite_note-47] The album generated two US [*Billboard* Hot 100](423161.md) top-ten singles: "[Borderline](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Borderline_\(Madonna_song\))" and "[Lucky Star](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lucky_Star_\(Madonna_song\))". In late 1983, Madonna's new manager, [Freddy DeMann](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Freddy_DeMann), arranged a meeting with film producer [Jon Peters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jon_Peters), who offered her the role of a club singer in the romantic drama *[Vision Quest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vision_Quest_\(film\))* (1985).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMorton2001121-48]

### 1984–1987: *Like a Virgin*, first marriage, *True Blue*, and *Who's That Girl*

Black and white photo of a young Madonna performing

Madonna performing in 1985 during [the Virgin Tour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Virgin_Tour)

In January 1984, Madonna gained further exposure with performances on *[American Bandstand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Bandstand)* and *[Top of the Pops](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Top_of_the_Pops)*.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBego200091O'Brien2007112-49] Wanting the material on her second studio album, *[Like a Virgin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Like_a_Virgin_\(album\))*, to be "stronger" than that of her debut, she selected all of the songs herself, five of which she wrote or co-wrote.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBego200094–102-50] *Like a Virgin* was released on November 12, 1984,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPerone2018112Taraborrelli200286-51] and became her first number-one album in Germany, Italy, the Netherlands, New Zealand, Spain, the UK and the US.[^cite_note-z407-52] In the lattermost nation, the album remained atop the *Billboard* 200 for three weeks and is the first album by a woman to sell over five million copies.[^cite_note-e322-53][^cite_note-54] *Like a Virgin* has sold over 21 million copies worldwide, making it one of the [best-selling albums](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_best-selling_albums).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPerone2018112-55][^cite_note-LAVWoldWide-56]

The album's title track, "[Like a Virgin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Like_a_Virgin_\(song\))", was selected as its lead single.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETaraborrelli200288-57] It was her first number-one on the *Billboard* Hot 100, topping the chart for six consecutive weeks.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESmith2009179-58][^cite_note-hot100-59] "Like a Virgin" attracted the attention of conservative organizations, who complained that the song and its accompanying video promoted premarital sex and undermined family values; [moralists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moralist) sought to have the song and video banned.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECross200731Voller199918-60] Madonna attracted significant media attention for her performance of the song at the inaugural [MTV Video Music Awards in 1984](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1984_MTV_Video_Music_Awards). Dressed in a wedding gown and white gloves, she appeared atop a large wedding cake before moving across the stage in a provocative manner.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEShonkMcClure2017183Taraborrelli200288-61] [MTV News](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MTV_News) later described the performance as one of the most iconic in pop music history.[^cite_note-62]

"[Material Girl](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Material_Girl)" was *Like a Virgin*'s second single and peaked at number two in the US.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESmith2009179Taraborrelli200289-63] While filming its music video, Madonna began a relationship with actor [Sean Penn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sean_Penn) and they married on her twenty-seventh birthday in 1985.[^cite_note-j885-64][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGuilbert200278Gnojewski200752-65] In *Vision Quest* (1985), Madonna appears as a nightclub singer and performs "[Crazy for You](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crazy_for_You_\(Madonna_song\))"—her second *Billboard* Hot 100 number-one single.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBego2000120-66][^cite_note-w440-67] That same year, she starred as the titular character in the comedy *[Desperately Seeking Susan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Desperately_Seeking_Susan)*, depicting a free-spirited [bohemian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bohemianism) drifter whose path intersects with a bored housewife through personal ads.[^cite_note-g263-68] The film included the song "[Into the Groove](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Into_the_Groove)", her first number-one single on the [UK singles chart](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UK_singles_chart).[^cite_note-BBC-69] *Desperately Seeking Susan* was named one of the ten best films of 1985 by [Vincent Canby](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vincent_Canby), a *[The New York Times](30680.md)* film critic.[^cite_note-70]

In April 1985, Madonna began her first North American concert series, [the Virgin Tour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Virgin_Tour).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBego2000126,_129-71] It coincided with the height of the [Madonna wannabe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madonna_wannabe) phenomenon, as many of her young female fans adopted [her fashion style](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fashion_of_Madonna).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMoore2015113-72] Styled by [Maripol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maripol), her look—which included lace tops, skirts over [capri pants](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Capri_pants), [fishnet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fishnet) stockings, [crucifix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crucifix) jewelry, stacked bracelets, and bleached hair—became widely associated with 1980s female fashion trends.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEClerk200220Voller199922-73][^cite_note-74] Around this time, Madonna released two additional singles for *Like a Virgin*, "[Angel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Angel_\(Madonna_song\))" and "[Dress You Up](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dress_You_Up)", both of which reached the top five of the *Billboard* Hot 100.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGeorge-WarrenRomanowskiPareles2001595–596-75] In July 1985, *[Penthouse](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Penthouse_\(magazine\))* and *Playboy* published nude photographs of Madonna taken in 1978, when she worked as an [art model](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Art_model).[^cite_note-76] She had posed for the images for modest pay, reportedly \$25 per session; the photographs were later sold for up to \$100,000.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMorton2001135-77] The publication caused significant media attention, though Madonna remained unapologetic.[^cite_note-w578-78]

A blonde woman onstage with curly hair holding a microphone, wearing a black dress

Madonna performing during the 1987 [Who's That Girl World Tour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Who's_That_Girl_World_Tour)

Madonna co-wrote and co-produced every track on her third studio album, *[True Blue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/True_Blue_\(album\))*.[^cite_note-peterPiatkowski-79] Inspired by and dedicated to her husband Penn, the album was released on June 30, 1986, to critical acclaim.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEO'Brien2007101–104Bego2000166-80] It topped the charts in an unprecedented 28 countries worldwide, including the US, where it remained at number one on the *Billboard* 200 for five weeks.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMcFarlan1989152-81][^cite_note-e732-82] All five singles released from the album reached the top five on the *Billboard* Hot 100; "[Live to Tell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Live_to_Tell)", "[Papa Don't Preach](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Papa_Don't_Preach)", and "[Open Your Heart](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Open_Your_Heart_\(Madonna_song\))" made Madonna the second female artist to have three songs from a single album top the chart.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBego2000186–189-83][^cite_note-84] "[True Blue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/True_Blue_\(Madonna_song\))" and "[La Isla Bonita](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/La_Isla_Bonita)" also topped the [European singles chart](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Hot_100_Singles); the latter became the continent's [biggest single of 1987](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_European_number-one_hits_of_1987).[^cite_note-85][^cite_note-86] *True Blue* was the best-selling album of 1986, the best-selling album of the 1980s by a female artist, and one of the best-selling albums in history, with 25 million copies sold globally.[^cite_note-q979-87][^cite_note-88]

Madonna appeared in the film *[Shanghai Surprise](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shanghai_Surprise)* (1986) alongside Penn, which was critically unsuccessful and earned her the [Golden Raspberry Award for Worst Actress](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Golden_Raspberry_Award_for_Worst_Actress).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBego2000165-89][^cite_note-f123-90] She made her stage debut the same year in [David Rabe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Rabe)'s *Goose and Tom-Tom*, which also starred Penn.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGuilbert200235-91] In 1987, she starred in the film *[Who's That Girl](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Who's_That_Girl_\(1987_film\))* and contributed four songs to [its soundtrack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Who's_That_Girl_\(soundtrack\)), including "[Who's That Girl](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Who's_That_Girl_\(Madonna_song\))" and "[Causing a Commotion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Causing_a_Commotion)", which peaked at numbers one and two on the *Billboard* Hot 100, respectively.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBego2000198-92] In June, she embarked on the [Who's That Girl World Tour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Who's_That_Girl_World_Tour), which broke several attendance records, including a performance near Paris attended by over 130,000 people—the [highest for a female artist at the time](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_highest-attended_concerts).[^cite_note-showstealer-93][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEVoller199929-94] In 1987, she released *[You Can Dance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/You_Can_Dance)*, a [remix album](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Remix_album) with reworked versions of songs from her earlier releases.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMorton200137-95] After a turbulent two-year marriage, Madonna filed for divorce from Penn on December 4, 1987, though she later withdrew the petition.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBego2000200–201-96]

### 1988–1991: *Like a Prayer*, *Dick Tracy*, and *The Immaculate Collection*

Madonna made her [Broadway theatre](725252.md) debut in the play *[Speed-the-Plow](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Speed-the-Plow)* at the [Royale Theatre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royale_Theatre), running from May to August 1988.[^cite_note-97] According to the [Associated Press](18935732.md), she filed an assault report against Penn following an alleged incident at their [Malibu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malibu,_California) residence over New Year's weekend.[^cite_note-98] Madonna filed for divorce on January 5, 1989, and reportedly requested the following week that no criminal charges be pursued.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBego2000214-99] That month, Madonna signed an endorsement agreement with soft-drink company [Pepsi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pepsi).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETaraborrelli2002165-100] In a commercial for the company, she premiered the song "[Like a Prayer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Like_a_Prayer_\(song\))", whose accompanying music video featured [Catholic imagery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christian_symbols) such as [stigmata](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stigmata) and [cross burning](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cross_burning), as well as a dream sequence depicting intimacy with a saint, prompting condemnation from the [Vatican](13393.md).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELofton2017132Bego2000224-101][^cite_note-102] Religious organizations called for a boycott of Pepsi products, leading the company to withdraw the commercial and terminate her sponsorship deal.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBego2000224–225Taraborrelli2002174-103]

A blond woman looking down

Madonna at the AIDS Project Los Angeles benefit concert in 1990

"Like a Prayer" was released as the lead single from her fourth studio album, also titled [*Like a Prayer*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Like_a_Prayer_\(album\)). The song became her seventh number-one single on the *Billboard* Hot 100 and spent three weeks atop the chart.[^cite_note-h637-104] Madonna co-wrote and co-produced it alongside [Patrick Leonard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Patrick_Leonard), Stephen Bray, and [Prince](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prince_\(musician\)).[^cite_note-q879-105] In the US, *Like a Prayer* was released on March 21, 1989, to universal acclaim from critics. *[Rolling Stone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rolling_Stone)*'s [J. D. Considine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/J._D._Considine) described it as "proof not only that Madonna should be taken seriously as an artist but that hers is one of the most compelling voices of the Eighties".[^cite_note-u374-106] *Like a Prayer* spent six weeks atop the *Billboard* 200—the longest of any of her albums—and eventually sold 15 million copies worldwide.[^cite_note-talkslikeaprayer-107][^cite_note-108] The singles "[Express Yourself](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Express_Yourself_\(Madonna_song\))" and "[Cherish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cherish_\(Madonna_song\))" both reached number two in the US, while "[Keep It Together](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Keep_It_Together_\(song\))" peaked within the top ten.[^cite_note-talkslikeaprayer-107] *Billboard* and MTV named Madonna "Artist of the Decade" (1980s).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGabriel2023298-109][^cite_note-110]

In April 1990, she launched the [Blond Ambition World Tour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blond_Ambition_World_Tour).[^cite_note-q984-111] *Rolling Stone* described it as an "elaborately choreographed, sexually provocative extravaganza" and named it the best tour of 1990.[^cite_note-112] It drew criticism from religious organizations, particularly for her performance of "Like a Virgin", in which she simulated [masturbation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Masturbation) while being caressed by two male dancers.[^cite_note-showstealer-93] Madonna defended the show, stating that it was intended for "open minds" and encouraged audiences to view sexuality differently.[^cite_note-carrie-113] [The live recording of the tour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blond_Ambition_World_Tour_Live) earned Madonna the [Grammy Award for Best Long Form Music Video](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grammy_Award_for_Best_Long_Form_Music_Video).[^cite_note-r210-114] Her first documentary film, *[Truth or Dare](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Truth_or_Dare_\(documentary\))* (titled *In Bed with Madonna* outside North America), was released in May 1991.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETaraborrelli2002178,_214-115][^cite_note-d504-116] Chronicling her Blond Ambition World Tour, it became the highest-grossing documentary ever at the time, a record it held until it was surpassed eleven years later.[^cite_note-117]

Madonna portrayed [Breathless Mahoney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Breathless_Mahoney) in the film *[Dick Tracy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dick_Tracy_\(1990_film\))* (1990), directed by and starring [Warren Beatty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Warren_Beatty) in the title role. The film [topped the US box office](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_1990_box_office_number-one_films_in_the_United_States) for two consecutive weeks, and [Owen Gleiberman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Owen_Gleiberman) of *[Entertainment Weekly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Entertainment_Weekly)* praised Madonna's performance, saying that he wished she had been given more screen time.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETaraborrelli2002150–153Bego2000253–254-118][^cite_note-t317-119] To accompany the film, she released the soundtrack album *[I'm Breathless](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/I'm_Breathless)*, with songs largely inspired by 1930s music.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTERooksby200437,_70-120] It spawned the single "[Vogue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vogue_\(song\))", which topped the charts in more than 30 countries, including the US, where it peaked at number one on the Hot 100 for three weeks.[^cite_note-yahoo-121][^cite_note-t028-122] During production, Madonna and Beatty began a relationship that ended shortly after the film's premiere.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBego2000233–238-123] In October 1990, she recorded a [public service announcement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Public_service_announcement) supporting [Rock the Vote](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rock_the_Vote)'s voter registration campaign.[^cite_note-r707-124]

Madonna's first [greatest-hits compilation album](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greatest_hits_album), *[The Immaculate Collection](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Immaculate_Collection)*, was released in November 1990.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETaraborrelli2002202–206-125] The album has sold 30 million copies worldwide, making it the best-selling compilation album by a solo artist and one of the best-selling albums in history.[^cite_note-126][^cite_note-127] The set included two new songs, "[Justify My Love](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Justify_My_Love)" and "[Rescue Me](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rescue_Me_\(Madonna_song\))".[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBego2000260-128] Model [Tony Ward](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tony_Ward_\(model\)), Madonna's then-boyfriend, co-starred in the music video for "Justify My Love", which included imagery of [sadomasochism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sadomasochism), [bondage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bondage_\(BDSM\)), same-sex intimacy, and brief nudity.[^cite_note-r702-129][^cite_note-b547-130] Considered too sexually explicit, the video was banned by MTV.[^cite_note-rebel-131] The resulting controversy contributed to the song reaching number one on the *Billboard* Hot 100, becoming her ninth chart-topping single.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBego2000260-128]

### 1992–1997: Maverick, *Erotica*, *Sex*, *Bedtime Stories*, *Evita*, and motherhood

In 1992, Madonna appeared in the film *[A League of Their Own](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/A_League_of_Their_Own)* as a member of an all-women's baseball team.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBego2000274O'Brien2007535-132] The film reached [number one at the US box office](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_1992_box_office_number-one_films_in_the_United_States) in its second weekend of release.[^cite_note-p198-133] Madonna also recorded its theme song, "[This Used to Be My Playground](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/This_Used_to_Be_My_Playground)", which became her tenth *Billboard* Hot 100 number-one single, the most for a woman at the time.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBego2000281-134][^cite_note-r026-135] In April 1992, Madonna established the entertainment company [Maverick](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maverick_\(entertainment_company\)), which included a record label, film production unit, and divisions for music publishing, television, book publishing, and merchandising. The venture was a partnership with [Time Warner](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Time_Warner) and provided her with a \$60 million advance and a 20 percent royalty rate—the highest in the industry at the time.[^cite_note-NYT_-_\$60_million-136] Maverick went on to become one of the most successful [artist-run labels](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vanity_label) in history, signing successful acts such as [Alanis Morissette](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alanis_Morissette) and [Michelle Branch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michelle_Branch).[^cite_note-137]

Madonna with short blonde hair, wearing a green bra and purple pants, singing to a microphone, held in her left hand.

Madonna performing at [the Girlie Show](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Girlie_Show_\(Madonna\)) in 1993

Later in 1992, Madonna co-sponsored the first museum retrospective of her former partner, artist Jean-Michel Basquiat, at the [Whitney Museum of American Art](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Whitney_Museum_of_American_Art) in New York.[^cite_note-138] She released her fifth studio album, *[Erotica](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Erotica_\(album\))*, on October 20, 1992, in the US, and published her [coffee table book](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coffee_table_book) *[Sex](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sex_\(book\))* a day later.[^cite_note-BBEroticaTrackbyTrack-139][^cite_note-FOOTNOTETaraborrelli2002220–225-140] *Sex* featured sexually provocative and explicit images photographed by [Steven Meisel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Steven_Meisel) and drew strong negative reactions from critics, the general public, and conservative, [feminist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Feminist) and [anti-porn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-porn) groups; initial interest propelled the book to the top of [*The New York Times* Best Seller list](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_New_York_Times_Best_Seller_list).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETaraborrelli2002220,_224Bego2000283–285-141] A writer from *[The Washington Post](102226.md)* described it as an "oversized, overpriced, \[and boring] coffee table book of hardcore sexual fantasies".[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETaraborrelli2002224-142]

The widespread backlash overshadowed *Erotica*, which became her lowest-selling album at the time.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEO'Brien2007267Taraborrelli2002225-143] It reached number two on the US *Billboard* 200, and received favorable reviews from critics.[^cite_note-BBEroticaTrackbyTrack-139] The singles "[Erotica](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Erotica_\(song\))" and "[Deeper and Deeper](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deeper_and_Deeper)" reached the top ten of the *Billboard* Hot 100 and number one in Italy.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBego2000287-144][^cite_note-musicaedischi-145] During this period, Madonna was in a relationship with rapper [Vanilla Ice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vanilla_Ice), to whom she proposed.[^cite_note-146] The pair separated following the release of *Sex*, with Vanilla Ice claiming he had been included in the book without his consent.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEO'Brien2007249-147] Her performances in the 1993 films *[Body of Evidence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Body_of_Evidence_\(1993_film\))* and *[Dangerous Game](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dangerous_Game_\(1993_film\))* were poorly received. The former featured scenes of sadomasochism and bondage, while she played the abused wife of a filmmaker in the latter.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBego2000289O'Brien200770,_275-148][^cite_note-d947-149][^cite_note-r803-150]

Madonna launched her fourth concert tour, [the Girlie Show](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Girlie_Show_\(concert_tour\)), in September 1993, during which she appeared as a whip-cracking [dominatrix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dominatrix) and performed alongside topless dancers.[^cite_note-showstealer-93] She made [a highly publicized appearance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madonna_on_the_Late_Show_with_David_Letterman_in_1994) on the *[Late Show with David Letterman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Late_Show_with_David_Letterman)* in March 1994, using profanity that required broadcast censorship and handing Letterman a pair of her underwear while urging him to smell it. The sexually explicit releases of *Sex* and *Erotica*, along with her film roles and controversial Letterman appearance, prompted commentators to question her dependence on provocative imagery.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETaraborrelli2002232–235-151] Madonna briefly dated rapper [Tupac Shakur](31341.md) and basketball player [Dennis Rodman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dennis_Rodman) around this time.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEO'Brien2007302–303-152] Biographer [J. Randy Taraborrelli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/J._Randy_Taraborrelli) viewed Madonna's ballad "[I'll Remember](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/I'll_Remember)"—recorded for the film *[With Honors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/With_Honors_\(film\))* (1994)—as an effort to soften her provocative image. It peaked at number two on the *Billboard* Hot 100.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETaraborrelli2002235–236,_242-153]

With her sixth studio album, *[Bedtime Stories](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bedtime_Stories_\(album\))*, Madonna embraced a gentler sound and image to regain public favor.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETaraborrelli2002235–236,_242-153] Released in the US on October 25, 1994, the album debuted at number three on the *Billboard* 200 and produced the singles "[Secret](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Secret_\(Madonna_song\))" and "[Take a Bow](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Take_a_Bow_\(Madonna_song\))", which peaked at numbers three and one on the *Billboard* Hot 100, respectively. "Take a Bow" spent seven weeks atop the chart, making it her longest-running number-one.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBego2000293–294-154] Madonna sponsored the first major retrospective of [Tina Modotti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tina_Modotti)'s work at the [Philadelphia Museum of Art](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philadelphia_Museum_of_Art) in 1995,[^cite_note-155] and later supported exhibitions of Basquiat's paintings at London's [Serpentine Gallery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serpentine_Gallery) and [Cindy Sherman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cindy_Sherman) at New York City's [Museum of Modern Art](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Museum_of_Modern_Art).[^cite_note-156][^cite_note-157] In November 1995, Madonna released *[Something to Remember](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Something_to_Remember)*, a compilation album that comprises Madonna's ballads across the past decade. The record debuted at number six on the *Billboard* 200 and has sold ten million copies worldwide.[^cite_note-r064-158][^cite_note-Univision-159][^cite_note-160]

> This is the role I was born to play. I put everything of me into this because it was much more than a role in a movie. It was exhilarating and intimidating at the same time. And I am prouder of *Evita* than anything else I have done.

— Madonna talking about her role in *[Evita](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Evita_\(1996_film\))*

[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESt._Michael200467-161]

In February 1996, Madonna began filming the musical *[Evita](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Evita_\(1996_film\))* in Argentina.[^cite_note-162] Having long aspired to portray Argentine political leader [Eva Perón](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eva_Per%C3%B3n), she wrote to director [Alan Parker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alan_Parker) to express her interest in the role. After being cast, she undertook vocal training and studied Argentina's history and Perón's life. During production, Madonna experienced several bouts of illness related to pregnancy and the emotional demands of the film.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETaraborrelli2002246–256-163] Upon its release in December 1996, her performance was met with positive reviews from critics.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETaraborrelli2002285-164][^cite_note-evitany-165][^cite_note-msn-166] [Richard Corliss](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Richard_Corliss), writing for *Time*, described the film as well cast and visually impressive, lauding Madonna's ability to exceed expectations.[^cite_note-167] Her portrayal earned her the [Golden Globe Award for Best Actress in a Motion Picture – Musical or Comedy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Golden_Globe_Award_for_Best_Actress_in_a_Motion_Picture_%E2%80%93_Musical_or_Comedy).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBego2000314Guilbert200293Taraborrelli2002286-168]

The [*Evita* soundtrack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Evita_\(soundtrack\)), which peaked at number two on the *Billboard* 200, contained songs primarily performed by Madonna. These included "[You Must Love Me](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/You_Must_Love_Me)" and "[Don't Cry for Me Argentina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Don't_Cry_for_Me_Argentina#Madonna_version)", the latter of which topped the European singles chart.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETaraborrelli2002286-169][^cite_note-euro-170] On October 14, 1996, she gave birth to her daughter, [Lourdes "Lola" Maria Ciccone Leon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lourdes_Leon), with fitness trainer Carlos Leon.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBego2000307–310Taraborrelli2002260–262,_280-171] According to biographer [Mary Cross](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mary_Cross), Madonna—who had expressed concern that her pregnancy might jeopardize *Evita*—fulfilled significant personal goals: "Madonna had at last triumphed on screen and achieved her dream of having a child, both in the same year \[...] reinventing herself and her image with the public".[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECross200771-172] Her relationship with Carlos ended in May 1997, and she stated that they were "better off as best friends".[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETaraborrelli2002288Cross2007134-173]

### 1998–2002: *Ray of Light*, *Music*, second marriage, and touring comeback

After the birth of Lourdes, Madonna developed an interest in [Eastern mysticism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_mysticism) and [Kabbalah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kabbalah), introduced to her by actress [Sandra Bernhard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sandra_Bernhard).[^cite_note-174] This change in perception influenced her seventh studio album, *[Ray of Light](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ray_of_Light)*.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTERooksby200450O'Brien2007319-175] She collaborated with [electronic music](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electronic_music) producer [William Orbit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/William_Orbit) to create a fusion of dance, [pop](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pop_music), and [British rock](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_rock).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETaraborrelli2002302-176][^cite_note-wync-177] Music critic [Ann Powers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ann_Powers) said Madonna sought "a kind of lushness" for the album rather than the party-oriented sounds of 1990s [techno](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Techno) and [rave](43488.md) genres, opting instead for a more singer-songwriter approach that Orbit helped her achieve.[^cite_note-wync-177] *Ray of Light* was released on February 22, 1998.[^cite_note-v208-178] It debuted at number two on the *Billboard* 200 with the highest first-week sales for a woman at the time,[^cite_note-bb1998-179][^cite_note-RS-Titanic-180] topped the charts of 17 countries,[^cite_note-v757-181] and eventually sold over 16 million copies globally.[^cite_note-182] Critically acclaimed,[^cite_note-a461-183] *Ray of Light* is widely regarded as Madonna's *[magnum opus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Magnum_opus)*.[^cite_note-\:0-184][^cite_note-j666-185][^cite_note-e199-186]

The album's two highest-charting singles, "[Frozen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Frozen_\(Madonna_song\))" and "[Ray of Light](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ray_of_Light_\(song\))", peaked at numbers two and five on the US *Billboard* Hot 100, with the former becoming her first song to debut at number one in the UK.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETaraborrelli2002302Bego2000322-187][^cite_note-l361-188] At the [41st Annual Grammy Awards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/41st_Annual_Grammy_Awards), Madonna won four awards, including [Best Pop Album](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Best_Pop_Album) for *Ray of Light*, and [Best Dance Recording](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Best_Dance_Recording) and [Best Short Form Music Video](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Best_Short_Form_Music_Video) for the title track.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETaraborrelli2002304O'Brien2007332,_529–532-189] In 1998, Madonna established the [Ray of Light Foundation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ray_of_Light_Foundation), a non-profit for women, education, global development, and humanitarian aid.[^cite_note-190] She recorded the single "[Beautiful Stranger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beautiful_Stranger)" for the film *[Austin Powers: The Spy Who Shagged Me](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Austin_Powers:_The_Spy_Who_Shagged_Me)* (1999), which earned her a [Grammy Award for Best Song Written for a Motion Picture, Television or Other Visual Media](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grammy_Award_for_Best_Song_Written_for_a_Motion_Picture,_Television_or_Other_Visual_Media).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETaraborrelli2002304-191] She starred as a yoga teacher in the comedy-drama *[The Next Best Thing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Next_Best_Thing)* (2000), which received negative reviews and opened at number two at the US box office.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEO'Brien2007337,_348-192] For the soundtrack, she recorded a cover of [Don McLean](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Don_McLean)'s "[American Pie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Pie_\(song\))", her ninth UK number-one single.[^cite_note-v925-193]

A blond woman sitting on a block of hay, playing guitar and singing in front of a microphone.

Madonna performing at the [Drowned World Tour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Drowned_World_Tour) in 2001

On September 18, 2000, Madonna released her eighth studio album, *[Music](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Music_\(Madonna_album\))*, to critical acclaim.[^cite_note-z661-194][^cite_note-195] She collaborated with French producer [Mirwais Ahmadzaï](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mirwais_Ahmadza%C3%AF), and expressed a preference for working with unconventional, lesser-known artists who "are making music unlike anyone else out there".[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEO'Brien2007338–343Taraborrelli2002328-196][^cite_note-197] The album reached number one in over twenty countries and sold four million copies within the first ten days of release.[^cite_note-cnn-198] In the US, *Music* debuted atop the *Billboard* 200, becoming her first number-one album in eleven years since *Like a Prayer*.[^cite_note-199] The singles "[Music](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Music_\(Madonna_song\))" and "[Don't Tell Me](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Don't_Tell_Me_\(Madonna_song\))" peaked at numbers one and four on the *Billboard* Hot 100, respectively; the former became her twelfth song to peak atop the chart.[^cite_note-hot100-59][^cite_note-m272-200] Since its release, *Music* has sold over 11 million units worldwide.[^cite_note-wwsales-201]

Madonna met director [Guy Ritchie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guy_Ritchie) in mid-1998 at a dinner party hosted by mutual friends [Sting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sting_\(musician\)) and [Trudie Styler](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trudie_Styler).[^cite_note-202][^cite_note-203] She gave birth to their son, Rocco John Ritchie, in Los Angeles on August 11, 2000.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGuilbert2002106Taraborrelli2002310-204] She and Rocco experienced complications during the birth due to her [placenta praevia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Placenta_praevia).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECross200788-205] He was christened at [Dornoch Cathedral](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dornoch_Cathedral) in Scotland on December 21, 2000, and Madonna married Ritchie the following day at nearby [Skibo Castle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Skibo_Castle).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETaraborrelli2002309–310-206] In June 2001, she launched the [Drowned World Tour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Drowned_World_Tour), her first tour in eight years. It visited cities across the US and Europe, becoming the highest-grossing tour of the year by a solo artist with \$75 million in revenue.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEO'Brien2007356–366-207][^cite_note-h538-208][^cite_note-grossdwt-209] That same year, she released her second greatest-hits album, *[GHV2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GHV2)*, featuring a selection of her successful songs from the 1990s onward. It debuted at number seven in the US and sold seven million copies worldwide.[^cite_note-210][^cite_note-usdebut-211]

In 2002, Madonna starred in Ritchie's *[Swept Away](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Swept_Away_\(2002_film\))*, a remake of [Lina Wertmüller](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lina_Wertm%C3%BCller)'s [1974 film](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Swept_Away_\(1974_film\)).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEO'Brien2007366–368-212] The film was commercially unsuccessful and universally panned,[^cite_note-f730-213][^cite_note-i653-214] with [A. O. Scott](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/A._O._Scott), writing for *The New York Times*, stating that "a role like this one requires the surrender of emotional control, something Madonna seems constitutionally unable to achieve".[^cite_note-v146-215] In May 2002, she appeared in the [West End](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/West_End_theatre) play *[Up for Grabs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Up_for_Grabs_\(play\))* at the [Wyndhams Theatre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wyndhams_Theatre), which was poorly received by critics.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEO'Brien2007368,_537-216][^cite_note-p597-217][^cite_note-218] Later that year, Madonna released "[Die Another Day](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Die_Another_Day_\(song\))", the theme song for the [James Bond film of the same name](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Die_Another_Day), in which she also had a [cameo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cameo_appearance) role that [Peter Bradshaw](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peter_Bradshaw) of *[The Guardian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Guardian)* described as "incredibly wooden".[^cite_note-219][^cite_note-k446-220] The song reached number eight on the *Billboard* Hot 100,[^cite_note-hot100-59] and was nominated for the [Golden Globe Award for Best Original Song](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Golden_Globe_Award_for_Best_Original_Song) and two Grammy Awards.[^cite_note-221]

### 2003–2006: *American Life* and *Confessions on a Dance Floor*

Madonna and her backup dancers in military costumes performing onstage with their right hands held upright into a fist.

Madonna performing at the [Re-Invention World Tour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Re-Invention_World_Tour) in 2004

In 2003, Madonna worked with fashion photographer [Steven Klein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Steven_Klein_\(artist\)) on an exhibition installation titled *X-STaTIC Pro=CeSS*. The project was presented from March to May at New York's [Deitch Projects](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deitch_Projects) gallery and was later shown internationally in a revised version.[^cite_note-222]

Madonna reunited with Ahmadzaï to produce her ninth studio album, *[American Life](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Life)*—a reflection of her views on American society. The record, released on April 21, 2003, was met with mixed reception and debuted atop the US *Billboard* 200.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEO'Brien2007343-223][^cite_note-americanlifeyearslater-224][^cite_note-225] *American Life* became her lowest-selling release, with worldwide sales of four million copies by 2005.[^cite_note-226] Four singles were released from the album: "[American Life](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Life_\(song\))", "[Hollywood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_\(Madonna_song\))", "[Nothing Fails](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nothing_Fails)", and "[Love Profusion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Love_Profusion)". "American Life" was its only track to enter the *Billboard* Hot 100, peaking at number 37.[^cite_note-americanlifeyearslater-224] The tracks fared better internationally; "American Life" topped the charts in Canada and Italy,[^cite_note-AllMusicCanada-227][^cite_note-italiancharts-228] while "Nothing Fails" and "Love Profusion" reached number one in Spain.[^cite_note-229][^cite_note-230] "American Life" and "Hollywood" both reached number two in the UK.[^cite_note-231]

The original music video of "American Life" caused controversy due to its violence and [anti-war movement](39766716.md) imagery, and was withdrawn after the [2003 invasion of Iraq](201936.md) began. Madonna voluntarily censored herself due to the political climate of the country.[^cite_note-232] Her performance at the [2003 MTV Video Music Awards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2003_MTV_Video_Music_Awards)—in which she kissed singers [Britney Spears](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Britney_Spears) and [Christina Aguilera](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christina_Aguilera) while performing "Hollywood"—was highly publicized.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEO'Brien2007383–384-233][^cite_note-x026-234] Shortly afterwards, Madonna collaborated with Spears on the single "[Me Against the Music](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Me_Against_the_Music)" which was released that September.[^cite_note-235] She then released *[Remixed & Revisited](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Remixed_&_Revisited)*, an extended play featuring new remixes of songs from *American Life* and the unreleased "Your Honesty".[^cite_note-ew-236] Madonna signed a contract with [Callaway Arts & Entertainment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Callaway_Arts_&_Entertainment) to write five children's books. The first, titled *[The English Roses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_English_Roses)*, was published in September 2003 and tells the story of four English schoolgirls struggling with envy and jealousy. The book became the fastest-selling children's picture book at the time, with all proceeds donated to a children's charity.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECross200797HortonSimmons2007196–198-237][^cite_note-238]

In March 2004, Madonna and Maverick filed a lawsuit against [Warner Music Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Warner_Music_Group) and its former parent company, Time Warner, alleging financial mismanagement and poor accounting practices that resulted in significant losses. Warner countersued, claiming Maverick had incurred substantial losses independently. The dispute was settled when Warner acquired the Maverick shares held by Madonna and Ronnie Dashev, making the company a wholly owned subsidiary of Warner Music. Madonna remained signed to Warner under a separate recording contract.[^cite_note-labelsuit-239][^cite_note-240] She launched the [Re-Invention World Tour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Re-Invention_World_Tour) in May, performing across North America and Europe.[^cite_note-NYTimes-241][^cite_note-y976-242] The tour became 2004's highest-grossing, earning over \$120 million, and was later chronicled in her documentary *[I'm Going to Tell You a Secret](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/I'm_Going_to_Tell_You_a_Secret)* (2005).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEO'Brien2007387–390,_437-243] In November 2004, she was inducted into the [UK Music Hall of Fame](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UK_Music_Hall_of_Fame) as one of its five founding members.[^cite_note-244]

Madonna wearing sunglasses and a disco-inspired bodysuit performing

Madonna performing at the [Confessions Tour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Confessions_Tour) in 2006

In January 2005, Madonna performed [John Lennon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Lennon)'s "[Imagine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Imagine_\(song\))" at [Tsunami Aid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tsunami_Aid) and appeared at the [Live 8](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Live_8) benefit concert in London six months later.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEO'Brien2007450–451-245][^cite_note-246] She initially worked with Ahmadzaï to produce her tenth studio album, *[Confessions on a Dance Floor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Confessions_on_a_Dance_Floor)*, but later turned to [Stuart Price](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stuart_Price) to attain the sound she was seeking.[^cite_note-madonnaconfessionsnov-247] Structured like a continuous [DJ mixed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/DJ_mix) club set, the album was released in the US on November 15, 2005, to critical acclaim.[^cite_note-madonnaconfessionsnov-247] Keith Caulfield, writing in *Billboard*, heralded the album as a "welcome return to form for the Queen of Pop".[^cite_note-bbalbum-248] *Confessions on a Dance Floor* topped the charts in a record-breaking 40 countries, won Madonna the [Grammy Award for Best Electronic/Dance Album](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grammy_Award_for_Best_Electronic/Dance_Album) at the [49th Annual Grammy Awards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/49th_Annual_Grammy_Awards), and sold over 10 million copies worldwide.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGlenday2006187-249][^cite_note-t281-250][^cite_note-WWsales-251]

The lead single, "[Hung Up](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hung_Up)", reached number one in a record-breaking forty-one countries. It sampled [ABBA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ABBA)'s "[Gimme! Gimme! Gimme! (A Man After Midnight)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gimme!_Gimme!_Gimme!_\(A_Man_After_Midnight\))", marking the second time the band permitted such use. ABBA songwriter [Björn Ulvaeus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bj%C3%B6rn_Ulvaeus) called it "a wonderful track—100 per cent solid pop music".[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGlenday2006187O'Brien2007426-252][^cite_note-b085-253] The album's second single, "[Sorry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sorry_\(Madonna_song\))", became Madonna's twelfth number-one single in the UK.[^cite_note-BBC-69] Madonna launched the [Confessions Tour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Confessions_Tour) in May 2006, which grossed more than \$193.7 million, making it the [highest-grossing tour by a woman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_highest-grossing_concert_tours_by_women) at the time.[^cite_note-BBGross-254] During the performance of "Live to Tell", she used religious imagery, including a crucifix and a [crown of thorns](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crown_of_thorns), prompting the [Russian Orthodox Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_Orthodox_Church) and the [Federation of Jewish Communities of Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federation_of_Jewish_Communities_of_Russia) to call for a boycott.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEChapman2010333-255]

While touring, Madonna founded the charitable organization [Raising Malawi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Raising_Malawi) and helped fund an orphanage in the country.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEO'Brien2007452–456-256] There, she decided to adopt a boy named David Banda in 2006. The adoption provoked public debate, as Malawian law required prospective parents to reside in the country for one year before adopting, a condition Madonna did not meet.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEO'Brien200718-257][^cite_note-258] Malawi's Minister of Women and Child Development, [Kate Kainja](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kate_Kainja), had also blocked officials from traveling to meet Madonna and Ritchie as potential adopters.[^cite_note-katek-259] Speaking on *[The Oprah Winfrey Show](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Oprah_Winfrey_Show)*, Madonna explained that Malawi had no formal adoption laws for foreigners and described how Banda had been suffering from [pneumonia](52135.md) after surviving [malaria](20423.md) and [tuberculosis](30653.md).[^cite_note-h980-260][^cite_note-261] Banda's father, Yohane, defended the adoption, saying he understood and supported the arrangement. The adoption was finalized in May 2008.[^cite_note-262][^cite_note-263]

### 2007–2011: Filmmaking, *Hard Candy*, and business ventures

Madonna wearing a loose dress with black and brown prints on it. She is looking to the right and smiling.

Madonna at the premiere of *[I Am Because We Are](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/I_Am_Because_We_Are)* in 2008

In July 2007, Madonna released and performed "[Hey You](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hey_You_\(Madonna_song\))" at the London [Live Earth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Live_Earth_concert,_London) concert.[^cite_note-264] She subsequently announced her departure from Warner Bros. Records and signed a ten-year, \$120 million [360 deal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/360_deal) with [Live Nation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Live_Nation).[^cite_note-265] She produced, wrote, and narrated *[I Am Because We Are](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/I_Am_Because_We_Are)* (2008), a documentary directed by Nathan Rissman that addressed social issues in Malawi.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMorgan2015362-266] That same year, she directed her first feature film, *[Filth and Wisdom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Filth_and_Wisdom)*, which follows three friends pursuing their ambitions.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMorgan2015346-267] Reviews were largely negative,[^cite_note-268] with *[The Times](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Times)* describing it as a commendable debut and *[The Daily Telegraph](23797577.md)* calling it a promising but imperfect first effort.[^cite_note-269][^cite_note-270] On March 10, 2008, Madonna was inducted into the [Rock and Roll Hall of Fame](58902.md), becoming the second female solo artist to achieve it in her first year of eligibility, after [Janis Joplin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Janis_Joplin) in 1995.[^cite_note-rsinducted-271][^cite_note-272]

Madonna worked with [Justin Timberlake](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Justin_Timberlake), [Timbaland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Timbaland), and [Pharrell Williams](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pharrell_Williams) to produce her eleventh studio album, *[Hard Candy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hard_Candy_\(Madonna_album\))*.[^cite_note-timbalandhot-273] Released on April 29, 2008,[^cite_note-j999-274] *Hard Candy* debuted atop the charts of 37 countries, including the US *Billboard* 200.[^cite_note-275][^cite_note-s506-276] The lead single, "[4 Minutes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/4_Minutes)", peaked at number three on the *Billboard* Hot 100, becoming her thirty-seventh top-ten entry in the US and surpassing [Elvis Presley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elvis_Presley)'s record for the most top-ten songs.[^cite_note-mariahmadonna-277] In the UK, "4 Minutes" extended her record as the female artist with the most number-one singles.[^cite_note-278] *Rolling Stone*'s Caryn Ganz described *Hard Candy* as "an impressive preview of her upcoming tour",[^cite_note-279] while [BBC](19344654.md) correspondent Mark Savage criticized it as "an attempt to capture the urban market".[^cite_note-280] She launched the [Sticky & Sweet Tour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sticky_&_Sweet_Tour) in August 2008, her first major venture with Live Nation. Grossing over \$408 million, it became the second [highest-grossing tour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_highest-grossing_concert_tours) in history and the highest-grossing tour by a solo artist.[^cite_note-The_Wall_Live-281][^cite_note-282]

In July 2008, Madonna's brother Christopher published the book *[Life with My Sister Madonna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Life_with_My_Sister_Madonna)*, which created a rift between the two due to its unauthorized release.[^cite_note-283] In October, Madonna filed for divorce from Ritchie, citing [irreconcilable differences](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Irreconcilable_differences).[^cite_note-284] Two months later, her spokesperson confirmed that a settlement had been reached, granting Ritchie between [£50–60 million](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_most_expensive_divorces) (\$68.49–82.19 million), including the couple's [London pub](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Punch_Bowl_\(Mayfair\)), residence, and [Wiltshire estate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ashcombe_House,_Wiltshire).[^cite_note-285] The marriage was formally dissolved through a *[decree nisi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Decree_nisi)* at the Principal Registry of the Family Division in [High Holborn, London](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/High_Holborn). Custody of their sons, Rocco and David, then aged eight and three, was shared between Ritchie's London home and Madonna's residence in New York, where they lived alongside Lourdes.[^cite_note-286][^cite_note-287] In May 2009, Madonna applied to adopt Chifundo "Mercy" James from Malawi, but the country's High Court denied the request on the grounds that she was not a resident.[^cite_note-mercyjames-288] She appealed the decision, and in June, the Supreme Court overturned the ruling and granted her permission to adopt her.[^cite_note-289]

Madonna, wearing a silver top hat while holding a black guitar. She is singing into a microphone

Madonna performing during the [Sticky & Sweet Tour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sticky_&_Sweet_Tour) in 2008

Madonna concluded her contract with Warner Records with the release of her third greatest-hits album, *[Celebration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Celebration_\(Madonna_album\))*, in September 2009. The compilation included two new tracks, along with thirty-four songs spanning her career with the label.[^cite_note-celebraterelease-290] The album reached number one in multiple countries, including Canada, Germany, Italy, and the UK.[^cite_note-291] She appeared at the [2009 MTV Video Music Awards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2009_MTV_Video_Music_Awards) to deliver a tribute to the deceased singer [Michael Jackson](14995351.md).[^cite_note-292] By the end of the decade, Madonna was recognized as the most successful singles artist of the 2000s in the US and the most-played artist of the decade in the UK.[^cite_note-293][^cite_note-294] *Billboard* ranked her as the third highest-grossing touring artist of the 2000s, with earnings exceeding \$801 million, over 6.3 million attendees, and 244 sold-out performances out of 248 shows.[^cite_note-295] In January 2010, Madonna performed at [Hope for Haiti Now: A Global Benefit for Earthquake Relief](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hope_for_Haiti_Now:_A_Global_Benefit_for_Earthquake_Relief) concert.[^cite_note-296]

Madonna's third live album, *[Sticky & Sweet Tour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sticky_&_Sweet_Tour_\(album\))*, was released in April 2010, debuting at number ten in the US.[^cite_note-p883-297] That year, she authorized the television series *[Glee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Glee_\(TV_series\))* to feature her entire music catalog, leading to [an episode](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Power_of_Madonna) composed exclusively of her songs.[^cite_note-EW-298] She also collaborated with her daughter Lourdes to launch the [Material Girl](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madonna_fashion_brands) clothing line, inspired by her 1980s fashion.[^cite_note-mgclothing-299] In October 2010, she founded the global fitness chain [Hard Candy Fitness](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hard_Candy_Fitness), and in November 2011 introduced a second lifestyle brand, [Truth or Dare](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Truth_or_Dare_by_Madonna), offering footwear, fragrances, lingerie, and accessories.[^cite_note-300][^cite_note-301] Her second directorial feature was *[W.E.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/W.E.)* (2011), a biographical drama about the relationship between [King Edward VIII](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/King_Edward_VIII) and [Wallis Simpson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wallis_Simpson).[^cite_note-302] The film received an unfavorable critical and commercial response.[^cite_note-303] [Its soundtrack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/W.E._\(soundtrack\)) included the [ballad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ballad) "[Masterpiece](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Masterpiece_\(Madonna_song\))", which earned Madonna a Golden Globe Award for Best Original Song.[^cite_note-c948-304]

### 2012–2016: Super Bowl XLVI halftime show, *MDNA*, and *Rebel Heart*

A group of performers onstage, with Madonna and Cee Lo Green at the front. They are all wearing black costumes with red and white stripes.

Madonna, joined by [Cee Lo Green](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cee_Lo_Green) and a [marching band](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marching_band), performing during the [Super Bowl XLVI halftime show](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Super_Bowl_XLVI_halftime_show) on February 5, 2012

In February 2012, Madonna headlined the [Super Bowl XLVI halftime show](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Super_Bowl_XLVI_halftime_show) at [Lucas Oil Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lucas_Oil_Stadium) in [Indianapolis, Indiana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indianapolis,_Indiana).[^cite_note-nfl12-305] Produced in collaboration with [Cirque du Soleil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cirque_du_Soleil) and choreographer [Jamie King](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jamie_King), the performance featured guest appearances by [LMFAO](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LMFAO), [Nicki Minaj](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nicki_Minaj), [M.I.A.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M.I.A._\(rapper\)) and [CeeLo Green](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CeeLo_Green). The broadcast drew 114 million viewers, making it the most-watched Super Bowl halftime show at the time, surpassing the viewership of the game itself.[^cite_note-t575-306][^cite_note-j172-307] Madonna primarily produced her twelfth studio album, *[MDNA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MDNA_\(album\))*, with Orbit and [Martin Solveig](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martin_Solveig).[^cite_note-308] It was released on March 26, 2012, to generally favorable critical reception.[^cite_note-o428-309][^cite_note-e296-310] *MDNA* was Madonna's first release under a three-album agreement with [Interscope Records](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interscope_Records), signed as part of her 360 deal with Live Nation, which could not distribute recorded music.[^cite_note-InterscopeDeal-311][^cite_note-312]

*MDNA* became her fifth consecutive studio album to debut at number one on the *Billboard* 200.[^cite_note-313] The lead single, "[Give Me All Your Luvin'](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Give_Me_All_Your_Luvin')", became her record-extending thirty-eighth top-ten single on the *Billboard* Hot 100.[^cite_note-314] She promoted the album with [the MDNA Tour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_MDNA_Tour), which began in May 2012 and addressed themes such as violence, human rights, and politics.[^cite_note-315][^cite_note-316] Grossing over \$305 million from 88 sold-out shows, it was the tenth highest-grossing tour and made Madonna the first woman to pass \$1 billion in touring revenue.[^cite_note-mdna_boxscore-317][^cite_note-rebelheartgross-318] Madonna collaborated with Steven Klein and directed a seventeen-minute film, *[secretprojectrevolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Secretprojectrevolution)*, which was released on [BitTorrent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BitTorrent_\(company\)) in September 2013.[^cite_note-w530-319] With the film, she launched the Art for Freedom initiative, which promotes art and free speech to combat global persecution and injustice. The project's website garnered over 3,000 submissions by January 2014.[^cite_note-320]

Madonna looking to her right while singing onstage, with her right hand on her waist.

Madonna performing at the [Rebel Heart Tour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rebel_Heart_Tour) in 2016

By 2013, Madonna's Raising Malawi had built ten schools educating 4,000 children in Malawi worth of \$400,000.[^cite_note-321] In May 2014, she donated to her [bankrupt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Detroit_bankruptcy) hometown of Detroit, and that year launched her [MDNA Skin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MDNA_Skin) care line in Tokyo.[^cite_note-322][^cite_note-skin-323] Madonna released her thirteenth studio album, *[Rebel Heart](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rebel_Heart)*, on March 10, 2015, three months after thirteen demos leaked online.[^cite_note-v918-324] She collaborated with a large range of producers, including [Avicii](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Avicii), [Diplo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Diplo), and [Kanye West](523032.md).[^cite_note-325][^cite_note-326] The central theme of the album was introspection along with "genuine statements of personal and careerist reflection".[^cite_note-slant3-327] Music critics responded positively towards *Rebel Heart*, calling it Madonna's best effort in a decade.[^cite_note-328]

Madonna began her [Rebel Heart Tour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rebel_Heart_Tour) in September 2015, which visited North America, Europe, Asia, and Australia, and grossed \$169.8 million from 82 shows.[^cite_note-329][^cite_note-330][^cite_note-rebelheartgross-318] While touring, Madonna became involved in a legal dispute with Ritchie regarding custody of their son Rocco. The conflict arose when Rocco chose to remain in England with Ritchie rather than continue traveling with Madonna. Court proceedings were held in both New York and London, but after several hearings, Madonna withdrew her custody application and opted to settle the matter privately.[^cite_note-331] In October 2016, *Billboard* named her its [Woman of the Year](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Billboard_Women_in_Music#Woman_of_the_Year_Award). Her candid and outspoken speech addressing [ageism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ageism) and [sexism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sexism) garnered extensive media attention.[^cite_note-332][^cite_note-woman2016-333]

In November 2016, she performed an impromptu acoustic concert at [Washington Square Park](745249.md) in support of [Hillary Clinton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hillary_Clinton)'s [2016 presidential campaign](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hillary_Clinton_2016_presidential_campaign).[^cite_note-impromptu-334] After [Donald Trump](4848272.md)'s election victory, Madonna delivered a speech at the [2017 Women's March](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_Women's_March) on Washington, where a remark about "blowing up the White House" drew controversy.[^cite_note-335][^cite_note-336] She later clarified that her comments had been taken out of context and emphasized that she was not advocating violence.[^cite_note-337]

### 2017–2021: Move to Lisbon and *Madame X*
