In February 2017, Madonna adopted four-year-old twin sisters, Estere and Stella, from Malawi, and relocated to [Lisbon](18091.md), Portugal, later that year with her children.[^cite_note-k866-338][^cite_note-339] That July, she inaugurated the [Mercy James Institute for Pediatric Surgery and Intensive Care](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mercy_James_Institute_for_Pediatric_Surgery_and_Intensive_Care) in Malawi, a hospital established by her Raising Malawi foundation.[^cite_note-340] Her live album [*Rebel Heart Tour*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rebel_Heart_Tour_\(album\)) was released in September 2017 and won Best Music Video for Western Artists at the 32nd [Japan Gold Disc Awards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Japan_Gold_Disc_Awards).[^cite_note-j792-341][^cite_note-342] That same month, she launched her skincare brand MDNA Skin in selected US stores.[^cite_note-343] Earlier in the year, the auction house Gotta Have Rock and Roll attempted to sell Madonna's personal items, including letters from Tupac Shakur. Although she sought legal action to halt the sale, the court ruled in favor of art dealer Darlene Lutz due to a 2004 agreement authorizing the sale of the items.[^cite_note-344]

Madonna singing onstage in front of a greenish backdrop, while wearing black costumes and an eye-patch.

Madonna performing at the [Madame X Tour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madame_X_Tour) in 2020

While residing in Lisbon, Madonna met Portuguese musician [Dino D'Santiago](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dino_D'Santiago), who introduced her to local artists performing [fado](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fado), [morna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Morna_\(music\)), and [samba music](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Samba_music). She frequently attended their informal "living room sessions", which inspired her fourteenth studio album, *[Madame X](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madame_X_\(album\))*.[^cite_note-345] The album was released on June 14, 2019, and Madonna produced it with various musicians, primarily her longtime collaborator Mirwais and [Mike Dean](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mike_Dean_\(music_producer\)).[^cite_note-u462-346][^cite_note-347] *Madame X* debuted at number one on the *Billboard* 200, her ninth to do so.[^cite_note-348] Its four singles—"[Medellín](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Medell%C3%ADn_\(song\))", "[Crave](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crave_\(Madonna_and_Swae_Lee_song\))", "[I Rise](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/I_Rise_\(song\))" and "[I Don't Search I Find](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/I_Don't_Search_I_Find)"—each reached number one on the *Billboard* Dance Club Songs chart, further extending her record for the most chart-topping entries.[^cite_note-DanceRecord-349]

Madonna appeared as the interval act at the [Eurovision Song Contest 2019](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eurovision_Song_Contest_2019) and performed "Like a Prayer", and then "[Future](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Future_\(Madonna_and_Quavo_song\))" with rapper [Quavo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quavo).[^cite_note-excruciatingeuro-350] Her [Madame X Tour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madame_X_Tour), an all-theater concert series held in select cities across North America and Europe, began in September 2019 and grossed over \$51.4 million in revenue.[^cite_note-e693-351][^cite_note-excruciatingeuro-350] That December, Madonna began a relationship with dancer Ahlamalik Williams, who had previously joined her Rebel Heart Tour in 2015.[^cite_note-l726-352] The tour later faced multiple cancellations due to Madonna's recurring knee injury and concluded prematurely on March 8, 2020, after the French government prohibited large gatherings because of the [COVID-19 pandemic](62750956.md).[^cite_note-353][^cite_note-354] Later that month, she drew criticism for posting a now-deleted video on [Instagram](31591547.md) and [Twitter](9988187.md); in it, she sat naked amid rose petals and candles, calling COVID-19 "the great equalizer" that makes everyone equal regardless of status.[^cite_note-t595-355][^cite_note-s638-356]

In April 2020, Madonna announced financial contributions to the COVID-19 Therapeutics Accelerator, led by the [Bill & Melinda Gates Foundation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bill_&_Melinda_Gates_Foundation), the [Wellcome Trust](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wellcome_Trust), and [Mastercard](378791.md), and donated an additional \$1 million to vaccine research.[^cite_note-357][^cite_note-358] A month later, Madonna revealed she had [tested positive](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/COVID-19_testing) for [coronavirus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Severe_acute_respiratory_syndrome_coronavirus_2) antibodies.[^cite_note-359] She and [Missy Elliott](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Missy_Elliott) contributed guest vocals to [Dua Lipa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dua_Lipa)'s single "[Levitating](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Levitating_\(song\))", featured on Lipa's 2020 remix album *[Club Future Nostalgia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Club_Future_Nostalgia)*.[^cite_note-360] Madonna then began developing a biographical film about her life, which she planned to direct.[^cite_note-Grobar-361] Screenwriters [Erin Cressida Wilson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Erin_Cressida_Wilson) and [Diablo Cody](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Diablo_Cody) worked on the script at different stages, and actress [Julia Garner](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Julia_Garner) was cast in the lead role before the project was postponed, and ultimately scrapped over budgeting and casting disagreements with [Universal Pictures](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Universal_Pictures).[^cite_note-Grobar-361][^cite_note-362][^cite_note-363][^cite_note-364] In October 2021, Madonna released *[Madame X](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madame_X_\(2021_film\))*, a [documentary film](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Documentary_film) about her concert tour of the same name, through [Paramount+](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+).[^cite_note-f041-365]

### 2022–present: Remix albums and *Confessions II*

In August 2021, coinciding with her 63rd birthday, Madonna announced her return to [Warner Records](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Warner_Records)—the rebranded successor to Warner Bros. Records—in a global partnership. The deal granted the label rights to her entire recorded music catalog, including her three most recent albums originally released under Interscope. As part of the agreement, she began a series of catalog reissues in 2022 to mark the fortieth anniversary of her recording career. The remix album *[Finally Enough Love: 50 Number Ones](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Finally_Enough_Love:_50_Number_Ones)* was released in August, following a 16-track abridged edition made available for streaming two months prior.[^cite_note-366] Featuring her 50 number-one songs on *Billboard*'s Dance Club Songs chart, the album celebrated Madonna's long-standing connection to dance music and became her 23rd top-ten entry on the *Billboard* 200.[^cite_note-367][^cite_note-368]

Madonna with long, wavy blonde hair singing into a microphone on stage.

Madonna performing during [the Celebration Tour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Celebration_Tour) in 2023

Between 2022 and 2023, Madonna issued several standalone singles, including "[Back That Up to the Beat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Back_That_Up_to_the_Beat)", a previously unreleased demo from the *Rebel Heart* sessions that went viral on social media.[^cite_note-v587-369][^cite_note-applemusic-370] She also contributed to three tracks on [Christine and the Queens](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christine_and_the_Queens)' album *[Paranoïa, Angels, True Love](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parano%C3%AFa,_Angels,_True_Love)* (2023), and collaborated with [the Weeknd](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Weeknd) and [Playboi Carti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Playboi_Carti) on the single "[Popular](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Popular_\(The_Weeknd,_Playboi_Carti_and_Madonna_song\))", featured on the soundtrack to the television drama *[The Idol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Idol_\(TV_series\))*.[^cite_note-p605-371][^cite_note-372] In January 2023, Madonna announced [the Celebration Tour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Celebration_Tour), her first greatest hits concert tour.[^cite_note-ultimategreatest-373] The tour was to begin in July, but a month prior, she was hospitalized after being found unresponsive at her New York City residence.[^cite_note-ultimategreatest-373][^cite_note-q644-374] She spent five days in intensive care and was placed in a medically induced coma for 48 hours due to a serious bacterial infection following a low-grade fever.[^cite_note-n106-375][^cite_note-y019-376]

Following her recovery, the Celebration Tour commenced in October 2023 in London, receiving widespread critical acclaim.[^cite_note-377][^cite_note-378][^cite_note-379] It concluded the following May with a free concert on [Copacabana Beach](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Copacabana_Beach) in [Rio de Janeiro](25936.md), which drew an audience of 1.6 million—setting records for both the [largest standalone concert in history and the highest attendance ever for a female artist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_most-attended_concerts) at the time.[^cite_note-380][^cite_note-record1-381] The tour grossed over \$225 million across eighty shows, making Madonna the first woman to surpass \$100 million in earnings from six separate concert tours.[^cite_note-onlywomantoacheieveboxscore-382] Around this time, Madonna experienced the deaths of several family members, beginning with that of her eldest brother, Anthony, in February 2023.[^cite_note-383] Her younger brother, Christopher, died in October 2024, two weeks after that of their stepmother, Joan.[^cite_note-relativesdie-384]

In 2025, Madonna released the remix album *[Veronica Electronica](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Veronica_Electronica)* and the extended play *[Bedtime Stories: The Untold Chapter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bedtime_Stories:_The_Untold_Chapter)*, both of which contain previously unreleased demos.[^cite_note-b806-385][^cite_note-386] In January 2026, she became the face of The One, a [Dolce & Gabbana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dolce_&_Gabbana) fragrance. Madonna covered [Patty Pravo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Patty_Pravo)'s 1968 single "[La bambola](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/La_bambola#Madonna_version)" as part of the campaign, which included a short film and photographs.[^cite_note-g516-387] Madonna reunited with Stuart Price for her fifteenth studio album, *[Confessions II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Confessions_II)*, which was released on July 3, 2026. A follow-up to *Confessions on a Dance Floor* (2005), the album was critically acclaimed and debuted atop the *Billboard* 200.[^cite_note-388][^cite_note-389][^cite_note-v988-390] By then, Madonna had joined [the Beatles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Beatles) as the only acts to score ten number-one hits on the *Billboard* 200, the *Billboard* Hot 100, the UK Albums Chart, and the UK singles chart.[^cite_note-391] In April, she joined [Sabrina Carpenter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sabrina_Carpenter) at the [Coachella](81092105.md) festival to perform "Vogue", "Like a Prayer", and a new duet, "[Bring Your Love](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bring_Your_Love)".[^cite_note-Coachella26-392] "Bring Your Love" was released as the album's first single that same month, debuting at number 74 on the *Billboard* Hot 100.[^cite_note-z900-393]

A short [musical film](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Musical_film) titled *[Confessions II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Confessions_II_\(film\))* accompanied the album and premiered in June.[^cite_note-394] After a surprise concert in [Times Square](31389.md) in New York City that month,[^cite_note-395] she released the album's second single, "[Love Sensation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Love_Sensation_\(Madonna_song\))", and a [FIFA](11049.md) version of *Confessions II* track "[Read My Lips](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Read_My_Lips_\(Madonna_and_Feid_song\))" with Colombian singer [Feid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Feid), included on the bonus edition of the *[Official FIFA World Cup 2026 Album](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Official_FIFA_World_Cup_2026_Album)*.[^cite_note-396] In July, she released "[Danceteria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Danceteria_\(song\))" as the third single of *Confessions II*. On July 19, Madonna performed "Music" and elements of "Danceteria" during the [2026 FIFA World Cup final halftime show](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_FIFA_World_Cup_final_halftime_show), which took place at the [New York New Jersey Stadium](5476983.md).[^cite_note-397] In August, she performed at the [AFAS Live](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AFAS_Live) in [Amsterdam](844.md) as a part of [WorldPride](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WorldPride) during the [Pride Amsterdam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pride_Amsterdam) and was joined onstage by [Kylie Minogue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kylie_Minogue) to sing a new version of "Love Sensation". The remix featuring Minogue was released as a single that month,[^cite_note-398] and became Madonna's [65th UK top-ten single](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_artists_with_the_most_UK_singles_chart_top_tens).[^cite_note-399] In September, Madonna released a new version of "Danceteria" featuring [Charli XCX](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charli_XCX), entitled "[Danceteria Afterhours](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Danceteria_\(song\)#Danceteria_Afterhours)".[^cite_note-400]

Madonna received thirteen nominations for the [2026 MTV Video Music Awards](82993690.md), tying the record for the most nominations for an artist in a single year.[^cite_note-401] She won seven of her nominations, including [Best Album](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MTV_Video_Music_Award_for_Best_Album) and [Artist of the Year](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MTV_Video_Music_Award_for_Artist_of_the_Year).[^cite_note-h216-402] Madonna also opened the awards ceremony performing "Bring Your Love" with Sabrina Carpenter and "Danceteria Afterhours" with Charli XCX.[^cite_note-403]

## Artistry

### Influences

Black-and-white photo of a woman with short, blonde hair

Black-and-white photo of a young woman

A man performing on stage holding a guitar

Madonna has been inspired by film stars such as [Marilyn Monroe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marilyn_Monroe) (left), artists such as [Frida Kahlo](162276.md) (center), and musicians like [David Bowie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Bowie) (right).

When initially starting her career, Madonna's primary musical inspirations were [Debbie Harry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Debbie_Harry) of [Blondie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blondie_\(band\)) and [David Bowie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Bowie), whose [Diamond Dogs Tour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Diamond_Dogs_Tour) concert was the first performance she ever attended.[^cite_note-\:1-404][^cite_note-q580-405][^cite_note-o084-406] Madonna has cited [Nancy Sinatra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nancy_Sinatra) as one of her idols and said that her song "[These Boots Are Made for Walkin'](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/These_Boots_Are_Made_for_Walkin')" (1965) left a strong impression on her.[^cite_note-hot-407] As a young woman, she sought to expand her artistry through literature, art, and music, developing a particular interest in [classical compositions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Classical_music).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESt._Michael200434–35-408] She identified [Baroque](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baroque_music) as her preferred style and admired [Wolfgang Amadeus Mozart](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wolfgang_Amadeus_Mozart) and [Frédéric Chopin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fr%C3%A9d%C3%A9ric_Chopin), praising the "feminine quality" in their work.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESt._Michael200435,_40-409] Among Madonna's other principal influences are [Chrissie Hynde](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chrissie_Hynde),[^cite_note-c632-410] [Aretha Franklin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aretha_Franklin),[^cite_note-411] [Patti Smith](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Patti_Smith),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGarber2025279-412] [Karen Carpenter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karen_Carpenter),[^cite_note-g366-413] [the Supremes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Supremes),[^cite_note-h269-414] Joni Mitchell,[^cite_note-d502-415] and [Led Zeppelin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Led_Zeppelin),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEEdmondson2013680-416] as well as dancers [Rudolf Nureyev](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rudolf_Nureyev) and Martha Graham, the latter of which she considers one of her biggest mentors.[^cite_note-\:1-404][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEO'Brien2007242,_284Edmondson2013680-417]

Madonna has also been inspired by the works of American writer [James Baldwin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Baldwin).[^cite_note-d690-418] As a child, Madonna drew inspiration from Hollywood actresses such as [Carole Lombard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carole_Lombard), [Judy Holliday](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Judy_Holliday), and [Marilyn Monroe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marilyn_Monroe), admiring their blend of femininity, strength, and sex appeal, and remarking that she "saw \[her]self in them".[^cite_note-hot-407] Her music video for "Material Girl" recreated Monroe's performance of "[Diamonds Are a Girl's Best Friend](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Diamonds_Are_a_Girl's_Best_Friend)" from the film *[Gentlemen Prefer Blondes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gentlemen_Prefer_Blondes_\(1953_film\))* (1953).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGuilbert200243Elias202412-419] In preparation for her role in *Who's That Girl*, Madonna studied the 1930s [screwball comedies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Screwball_comedies) of Lombard, whose style she sought to emulate.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETaraborrelli200285-420] The visual concept for her "Express Yourself" (1989) video drew influence from [Fritz Lang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fritz_Lang)'s silent film *[Metropolis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metropolis_\(1927_film\))* (1927),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGuilbert2002120–121-421][^cite_note-w381-422] while "Vogue" paid homage to the [golden age of Hollywood glamor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_glamour), referencing the photography of [Horst P. Horst](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Horst_P._Horst) and channeling the screen presence of [Marlene Dietrich](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marlene_Dietrich), Lombard, and [Rita Hayworth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rita_Hayworth).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEVictor200123-423]

> Historians, musicians, and anthropologists trace her influences—from African American gospel music to Japanese fashion, Middle Eastern spirituality to feminist art history—and the ways she borrows, adapts, and interprets them.

— [National Geographic Society](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Geographic_Society) on Madonna's influences.

[^cite_note-Natgeo-424]

Madonna also drew inspiration from the [visual arts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Visual_arts), particularly Mexican painter [Frida Kahlo](162276.md).[^cite_note-l919-425] The music video for "[Bedtime Story](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bedtime_Story_\(Madonna_song\))" (1995) incorporated imagery influenced by the works of Kahlo and [Remedios Varo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Remedios_Varo).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGabriel2023385-426] A collector of [Tamara de Lempicka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tamara_de_Lempicka)'s [Art Deco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Art_Deco) paintings, Madonna has featured them throughout her music videos and stage productions.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECross200747-427][^cite_note-s310-428] Her video for "Hollywood" (2003) paid tribute to the photography of [Guy Bourdin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guy_Bourdin), though it later prompted a lawsuit filed by Bourdin's son over the unauthorized use of his father's images.[^cite_note-j380-429] The sadomasochistic motifs in [pop artist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pop_artist) [Andy Warhol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andy_Warhol)'s [underground films](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Underground_film) were echoed in the videos for "Erotica" and "Deeper and Deeper".[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGuilbert200269-430]

Madonna's Catholic upbringing has remained a consistent influence throughout her career, from her use of the [rosary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rosary) as a fashion piece to her musical inputs, including albums such as *Like a Prayer* (1989).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEO'Brien2007126–131Fouz-HernándezJarman-Ivens200467–70-431] In 2011, she attended meetings and services at an [Opus Dei](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Opus_Dei) center, a Catholic institution that promotes sanctity through daily life.[^cite_note-p553-432] In a 2015 interview with *Rolling Stone*, Madonna remarked that she has always felt an "inexplicable connection" to Catholicism and stated that it consistently manifests throughout her work.[^cite_note-433]

### Musical style and composition

> I was in Barney's (store) getting a tie for the Grammys and I saw Madonna there. I introduced myself, and I told her that she didn't get the credit she deserved as a songwriter. Which is true, but it's also an awkward thing to tell a huge superstar! She was very cool and gracious. I ended up getting a meeting with her in L.A. a few weeks later.

— [Rick Nowels](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rick_Nowels), on co-writing with Madonna.

[^cite_note-434]

Madonna's work has been the subject of critical analysis and debate. [Robert M. Grant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robert_M._Grant_\(economist\)), author of *Contemporary Strategy Analysis* (2005), observed that her musical career is a continual process of experimentation with new sounds and images.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGrant20053-435] In *Pop Goes the Decade: The Eighties*, Thomas Harrison described her as "an artist who pushed the boundaries" of what a female performer could achieve, both visually and lyrically.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHarrison2017213-436] Professor Santiago Fouz-Hernández observed that, while not possessing a particularly powerful or wide-ranging voice, Madonna expanded her artistic range through diverse musical, lyrical, and visual styles, "all with the intention of presenting herself as a mature musician".[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFouz-HernándezJarman-Ivens200455–58-437]

*Rolling Stone* included Madonna at number 36 on its list of the "[100 Greatest Artists of All Time](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rolling_Stone's_100_Greatest_Artists_of_All_Time)" (2004).[^cite_note-c856-438] She has managed all aspects of her career; she writes and produces most of her recordings.[^cite_note-Rolling_Biography-439][^cite_note-music_credit-440] Her desire for control in creative decisions was evident as early as her debut album, during which she reportedly disagreed with producer Reggie Lucas over the final mixes. Warner Bros. Records later allowed her to produce her third studio album independently.[^cite_note-PopMatterLTT-441][^cite_note-FOOTNOTETaraborrelli200276,_117–119-442] According to Stan Hawkins, author of *Settling the Pop Score*, Madonna is among the few female artists to have entered the male-dominated space of music production, saying that she is aware of the historical exclusion of women and actively "has set out to change this".[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHawkins201759-443] Producer Stuart Price similarly remarked that "you don't produce Madonna, you collaborate with her \[...] she has her vision and knows how to get it".[^cite_note-444] Although referred to by the media and workers as a "control freak", Madonna has stated that she values creative input from those she works with.[^cite_note-445] She further explained:[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESt._Michael200440-446]

> I like to have control over most of the things in my career but I'm not a [tyrant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tyrant). I don't have to have it on my album that it's written, arranged, produced, directed, and stars Madonna. To me, to have total control means you can lose objectivity. What I like is to be surrounded by really talented, intelligent people that you can trust. And ask them for their advice and get their input.

Madonna developed her early songwriting skills while performing with the Breakfast Club in 1979, and was the sole writer of five tracks on her debut studio album.[^cite_note-r084-447][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBego200085-448][^cite_note-c884-449] As a songwriter, Madonna has registered more than 300 works with the [American Society of Composers, Authors and Publishers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Society_of_Composers,_Authors_and_Publishers), including 18 written entirely by herself.[^cite_note-450] *Rolling Stone* deemed her "an exemplary songwriter with a gift for [hooks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hook_\(music\)) and indelible lyrics",[^cite_note-451] and said that her compositions, though shaped by collaborations with producers across various genres, are consistently shaped by "her own sensibility and inflected with autobiographical detail".[^cite_note-RSsongwriters-452] Patrick Leonard, who co-wrote many of her songs, lauded her grasp of [melodic structure](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Melody_type) and said that "many times she's singing notes that no one would've thought of but her".[^cite_note-453] [*Spin*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spin_\(magazine\))'s Barry Walters similarly credited her songwriting for the consistency of her music.[^cite_note-454] Madonna was nominated for [induction](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Songwriters_Hall_of_Fame_inductees) into the [Songwriters Hall of Fame](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Songwriters_Hall_of_Fame) in 2014,[^cite_note-i776-455] 2016,[^cite_note-w138-456] and 2017,[^cite_note-h971-457] and was ranked number 56 on *Rolling Stone*'s list of the "[100 Greatest Songwriters of All Time](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rolling_Stone's_100_Greatest_Songwriters_of_All_Time)" (2015).[^cite_note-RSsongwriters-452]

Madonna's discography is often categorized as pop,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETaraborrelli200286,_242Bego20003,_319MetzBenson1999130,_162-460] [electronica](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electronica),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETaraborrelli2002301–302,_327–329-461][^cite_note-o819-462] and dance.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETaraborrelli2002129MetzBenson1999121-463] Her earliest work began with rock-oriented projects through the bands Breakfast Club and Emmy.[^cite_note-rock_star-464] With the latter, she recorded tracks that reflected the [punk rock](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Punk_rock) style of the early 1980s.[^cite_note-x394-465] She abandoned rock before signing with Gotham Records, which dropped her because of their dissatisfaction with her shift to [funk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Funk).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTERooksby20044-466] In his book *American Pop*, [Arie Kaplan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arie_Kaplan) referred to Madonna as "a pioneer" of [dance-pop](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dance-pop).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKaplan201731-467] Fouz-Hernández further observed that Madonna's consistent use of dance idioms and her connection with gay and sexually liberated audiences have often been undervalued in contrast to the perceived authenticity of [rock and roll](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rock_and_roll), noting that her music "refuses to be defined by narrow boundaries of gender, sexuality, or genre".[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFouz-HernándezJarman-Ivens200455–58-437]

The "cold and emotional" ballad "Live to Tell" and its parent album *True Blue* (1986) are regarded as Madonna's first significant musical reinvention.[^cite_note-peterPiatkowski-79][^cite_note-o063-468] *[PopMatters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PopMatters)* critic Peter Piatkowski described the song as a "deliberate effort to present Madonna as a mature and serious artist".[^cite_note-peterPiatkowski-79] Although she continued to record ballads alongside her more upbeat material, some of her albums, such as *Madonna* (1983) and *Confessions on a Dance Floor* (2005), are composed entirely of dance tracks.[^cite_note-hot100-59][^cite_note-469][^cite_note-470] With *Ray of Light* (1998), critics credited her with popularizing electronica and integrating it into mainstream pop music.[^cite_note-471][^cite_note-FOOTNOTETaraborrelli2002301-472] Her other stylistic departures include 1930s [big-band](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Big-band) [jazz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jazz) on *I'm Breathless* (1990);[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEO'Brien2007207-473] smooth [R\&B](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Contemporary_R\&B) arrangements on *Bedtime Stories* (1994);[^cite_note-474] [operatic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Opera) [show tunes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Show_tunes) on *Evita* (1996);[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEO'Brien2007307-475] [guitar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guitar)-based [folk music](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Folk_music) on *American Life* (2003);[^cite_note-476] and multilingual influences on *Madame X* (2019).[^cite_note-477]

### Voice and instruments

Madonna in a silver dress, playing electric guitar

Madonna playing the guitar [riff](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Riff) of "A New Level" by [heavy metal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heavy_metal_music) band [Pantera](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pantera) during the 2008 [Sticky & Sweet Tour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sticky_&_Sweet_Tour)

[^cite_note-478]

Madonna possesses a [mezzo-soprano](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mezzo-soprano) [vocal range](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vocal_range) and is self-conscious about her singing voice.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDean200334Fouz-HernándezJarman-Ivens200459–61-479][^cite_note-e199-186] [Mark Bego](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mark_Bego), author of *Madonna: Blonde Ambition*, described her as "the perfect vocalist for lighter-than-air songs" despite not being a "heavyweight talent".[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBego2000122-480] According to Tony Sclafani of [MSNBC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MSNBC), her vocal delivery reflects her "rock roots", explaining that, unlike most pop singers who perform songs "straight", Madonna incorporates subtext, [irony](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Irony), aggression, and other vocal idiosyncrasies similar to that of John Lennon and [Bob Dylan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bob_Dylan).[^cite_note-rock_star-464] In her early recordings, Madonna employed a bright, girlish timbre that she later tried to abandon after critics compared her voice to "[Minnie Mouse](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minnie_Mouse) on [helium](13256.md)".[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFouz-HernándezJarman-Ivens200459–61-481] While filming *Evita* (1996), she took voice training, which expanded her vocal versatility. Of the experience, she said, "I studied with a vocal coach for *Evita* and realized there was a whole piece of my voice I wasn't using. Before, I just believed I had a really limited range and was going to make the most of it."[^cite_note-vocal-459]

Madonna plays several musical instruments. She first learned to play the piano as a child.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMorton200147-482] During the late 1970s, she was taught drums and guitar by her then-boyfriend [Dan Gilroy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dan_Gilroy_\(musician\)), later joining his band, the Breakfast Club, as a drummer.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGnojewski200757-483] Madonna then played guitar with the group Emmy.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETaraborrelli200254-484] After her commercial breakthrough, she rarely performed instruments publicly until 2000, though she was credited with playing the [cowbell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cowbell_\(instrument\)) on *Madonna* (1983) and the [synthesizer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Synthesizer) on *Like a Prayer* (1989).[^cite_note-music_credit-440] In 1999, she undertook three months of [violin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Violin) training to prepare for a role as a violin teacher in the film *[Music of the Heart](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Music_of_the_Heart)* (1999), but withdrew from the project before filming.[^cite_note-485] She returned to performing with the guitar during promotion for *Music* (2000) with guidance from guitarist [Monte Pittman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monte_Pittman) to improve her technique.[^cite_note-486] Since then, Madonna has featured guitar performances in her studio recordings and concert tours.[^cite_note-music_credit-440][^cite_note-f016-487] She was nominated for the Les Paul Horizon Award at the 2002 [Orville H. Gibson Guitar Awards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gibson_Guitar_Corporation).[^cite_note-488]

### Music videos and performances

Further information: [Madonna videography](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madonna_videography)

In *The Madonna Companion*, biographers Allen Metz and Carol Benson observed that Madonna used MTV and the music video to leverage her popularity and enhance her recordings more effectively than any other contemporary pop artist. They noticed that many of her songs are closely tied to the imagery of their accompanying videos.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMetzBenson1999161-489] Cultural critic [Mark C. Taylor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mark_C._Taylor_\(philosopher\)) described the [postmodern art](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Postmodern_art) form [par excellence](https://en.wiktionary.org/wiki/par%20excellence) as the music video, declaring Madonna its "reigning queen" in his book *Nots* (1993).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETaylor1993191-490] He argued that "the most remarkable creation of MTV is Madonna", and that public reactions to her provocative videos were "predictably contradictory".[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETaylor1993191-490] Metz and Benson said that critical and public discussions surrounding some of her most controversial songs have often focused more on their videos and cultural impact than on the songs themselves.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMetzBenson1999161-489] According to Morton, "artistically, Madonna's songwriting is often overshadowed by her striking pop videos".[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMorton200115-491] Madonna was ranked as the greatest music video artist by MTV in 2003 and *Billboard* in 2020.[^cite_note-492][^cite_note-493]

Madonna dancing with a group of dancers wearing black outfits

Madonna in a jeweled black dress playing a ukulele while singing to a microphone

Madonna's live performances vary from choreographed routines such as [voguing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Voguing) (top) to stripped-down ones with only a [ukulele](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukulele) (bottom).

Madonna's early music videos fused American and Hispanic [street style](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Street_style) with flamboyant glamor. Through the visuals, she introduced her avant-garde [Downtown Manhattan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Downtown_Manhattan) fashion sensibility to an American audience.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMetzBenson1999161–163-494] The recurring use of Hispanic cultural motifs and Catholic symbolism continued into the *True Blue* era.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFouz-HernándezJarman-Ivens2004145-495] According to author [Douglas Kellner](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Douglas_Kellner), Madonna's embrace of "multiculturalism" and her "culturally transgressive moves" proved to be effective in appealing to a wide range of young audiences.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKellner1995271-496] Scholars have noted that through her visual narratives, Madonna often subverted traditional gender norms by symbolically reversing male dominance.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWelton199853–57-497] Her use of religious and racial imagery was prominent in the music video for "Like a Prayer", which depicted scenes of an African-American church choir, Madonna's attraction to a black saint statue, and her performance before burning crosses.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECross200770-498]

Madonna's acting has often been met with negative reviews from film critics.[^cite_note-filmcareerretro-499] Biographer [Andrew Morton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andrew_Morton_\(writer\)) noted that Madonna publicly dismissed the criticism.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMorton2001218-500] Following the critical and commercial failure of *Swept Away* (2002), she vowed never to act in another film.[^cite_note-b232-501] In a retrospective of her screen career titled *Body of Work* (2016) at New York's [Metrograph](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metrograph), *The Guardian*'s Nigel M. Smith argued that Madonna's struggles in film were largely due to the lack of quality roles available to her, stating that she could "steal a scene for all the right reasons".[^cite_note-filmcareerretro-499] Her concert tours often re-create her music videos; author Elin Diamond said that the ability to reproduce scenes from Madonna's videos in a live setting enhances the realism of the videos, arguing that "her live performances have become the means by which mediatized representations are naturalized".[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDiamond1996202-502]

According to Taraborrelli, Madonna's concerts, which feature multimedia elements, advanced technology, and elaborate sound design, function as "extravagant show piece\[s]" and "walking art show\[s]".[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETaraborrelli200290-503] Chris Nelson of *The New York Times* observed that artists like Madonna redefined standards of live performance with concerts "that included not only elaborate costumes and precision-timed [pyrotechnics](187584.md) but also highly athletic dancing"—often at the expense of live vocals.[^cite_note-sync-504] A writer for the *[Orlando Sentinel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Orlando_Sentinel)* said that she later restructured her stage shows to balance choreography and live singing by remaining stationary during vocally demanding sections while delegating more complex dance sequences to her backup performers.[^cite_note-v501-505] To support simultaneous singing and movement, Madonna began using a hands-free radio-frequency [headset microphone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Headset_\(audio\)), secured over the head or ears with a boom extending to the mouth.[^cite_note-506]

## Legacy

Main article: [Cultural impact of Madonna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cultural_impact_of_Madonna)

> She's a major [historical figure](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Historical_figure) and when she passes, the retrospectives will loom larger and larger in history.

— Academic [Camille Paglia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Camille_Paglia) on Madonna (2017).

[^cite_note-Paglia2017-507]

Madonna's profound influence on [popular music](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Popular_music) has led critics and scholars to refer to her as the "[Queen of Pop](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Honorific_nicknames_in_popular_music)".[^cite_note-508] Her legacy transcends music and has been examined by sociologists, historians, media scholars, and cultural critics, contributing to the development of [Madonna studies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madonna_studies), a subfield of American [cultural studies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cultural_studies).[^cite_note-509] *Rolling Stone* noted that Madonna's omnipresence across television, radio, magazines, and bookstores was an unparalleled pop dynamic not seen since [the Beatles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Beatles).[^cite_note-510] *The Daily Telegraph* remarked that Madonna "changed the world's social history",[^cite_note-511] with scholar Diane Pecknold noticing that "nearly every poll of the greatest or most influential figures in popular culture includes Madonna".[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHall2006\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidA7LOEAAAQBAJpgPA445_445–446\]-512] Madonna has frequently appeared in rankings of the greatest and most influential musicians.[^cite_note-513]

A wax figure with a long ponytail and large cone bra

Wax figure of Madonna at [Madame Tussauds](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madame_Tussauds_Hong_Kong) museum in Hong Kong

The [Rock and Roll Hall of Fame](58902.md) wrote that Madonna helped dissolve gender boundaries.[^cite_note-Rockhall-514] *Spin* wrote in 2020 that the title "Queen of Pop" scarcely captures her impact, stating that "she is Pop" and that she established "the blueprint for what a pop star should be".[^cite_note-515] Sclafani argued that before Madonna, most major music stars were male rock artists, and that her influence helped usher in an era where female solo performers became far more prominent in mainstream popular music.[^cite_note-516] Many female artists in the 21st century—including Britney Spears, [Beyoncé](83688.md), [Rihanna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rihanna), [Katy Perry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Katy_Perry), and [Lady Gaga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lady_Gaga)—have cited Madonna as an influence on their careers.[^cite_note-517] Madonna has also influenced male artists, inspiring rock frontmen [Liam Gallagher](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liam_Gallagher) of [Oasis](161515.md) and [Chester Bennington](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chester_Bennington) of [Linkin Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Linkin_Park) to become musicians.[^cite_note-z221-518][^cite_note-519]

[Madonna's use of sexual imagery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madonna_and_sexuality) has ignited widespread discussions regarding sexuality and [feminism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Feminism).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFouz-HernándezJarman-Ivens200481,_105,_165–168-520] Media scholar [John Fiske](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Fiske_\(media_scholar\)) remarked that the empowerment Madonna conveys is closely tied to the gratification of asserting control over self-definition, sexuality, and social dynamics.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFiske1989102-521] As discussed in *Doing Gender in Media, Art and Culture* (2009), Madonna's status as a female celebrity, performer, and [pop icon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pop_icon) continues to challenge and reinterpret established feminist discourses.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBuikemavan_der_Tuin2009119-522] According to lesbian feminist [Sheila Jeffreys](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sheila_Jeffreys), Madonna embodies what [Monique Wittig](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monique_Wittig) terms "the category of sex" as a site of power, while deliberately embracing the performance of women's assigned sexual labor.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEJeffreys200596-523] Similarly, communication scholar Sut Jhally has described her as "an almost sacred feminist icon".[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEJhally2006194-524]

Writers and scholars have also highlighted Madonna's engagement with [LGBTQ](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LGBTQ_culture), [Latino](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Culture_of_Latin_America), and [Black culture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/African-American_culture). [Matt Cain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Matt_Cain_\(writer\)) argued that her work helped bring [marginalized groups](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marginalized_groups) and cultures into mainstream popular culture.[^cite_note-v524-525][^cite_note-k560-526][^cite_note-h359-527] One author remarked that "by making culture generally available, Madonna becomes the culture of all social classes".[^cite_note-McMillanIndia-528] Canadian scholar [Karlene Faith](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karlene_Faith) similarly said that Madonna's peculiarity lies in her ability to "move freely across diverse cultural terrains", describing her as a "[cult figure](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cult_figure)" within multiple self-sustaining subcultures even as she achieved mainstream success.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFaith199788-529] She is often regarded as a [gay icon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madonna_as_a_gay_icon),[^cite_note-k067-530][^cite_note-i265-531] with [GLAAD](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GLAAD) president [Sarah Kate Ellis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sarah_Kate_Ellis) affirming that Madonna "always has and always will be the LGBTQ community's greatest [ally](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Straight_ally)",[^cite_note-Ally-532] and *[The Advocate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Advocate_\(magazine\))* characterizing her as "the greatest gay icon".[^cite_note-Advocate-533]

Madonna has also been studied as a [business figure and entrepreneur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Business_of_Madonna); she amassed over \$1.2 billion in sales during the first decade of her career.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKramaraeSpender20041408-534] According to Gini Gorlinski in *The 100 Most Influential Musicians of All Time* (2010), Madonna's authority and autonomy within the entertainment industry were unprecedented for a woman at the time.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGorlinski2010330-535] Scholars at the [London Business School](520187.md) described her as a "dynamic entrepreneur" whose success model is worth emulating, identifying her vision of success, understanding of the music industry, ability to recognize her own performance limits, work ethic, and adaptability as the basis for her commercial achievements.[^cite_note-LBS-536] Morton called Madonna "opportunistic, manipulative, and ruthless—someone who will not stop until she achieves her goals".[^cite_note-537]

Madonna has been criticized from social, moral and political perspectives.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESchwichtenberg199315–17McLeod201364–65-538][^cite_note-Bonita-539] Scholars have challenged her use of racialized and minority cultures and argued that her privileged position does little to aid the communities whose imagery she adopts.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEOleksyRydzewska2004136-540][^cite_note-541] Madonna has been linked to terms like "Madonna-economy" and "Madonnization", shorthand for [homogenizing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cultural_homogenization) American mass culture similar to that of [McDonaldization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/McDonaldization) and [Cocacolonization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cocacolonization).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBerleurBeardonLaufer1993\[httpswwwgooglecomaubookseditionFacing_the_Challenge_of_Risk_and_Vulnera9LEZAQAAIAAJhlengbpv1bsq22Madonna-economy22dq22Madonna-economy22printsecfrontcover_307\]Guilbert200287–88-542][^cite_note-Madonnization-543] Internationally, political and religious commentators have sometimes cast her as a symbol of Western [cultural hegemony](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cultural_hegemony) or "cultural terrorism", associating her with [Americanization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Americanization) and the erosion of local traditions.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELockard2011934–935Ruthven2004264CornwellStoddard200180-544][^cite_note-x947-545] Conservative groups, parental organizations and some philosophers have further criticized her as emblematic of mass culture's corrupting influence on youth, fueling [moral panics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moral_panic) over sexuality, religion and the commercialization of popular music.[^cite_note-546][^cite_note-547]

## Achievements

Main articles: [List of awards and nominations received by Madonna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_awards_and_nominations_received_by_Madonna) and [List of Madonna records and achievements](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Madonna_records_and_achievements)

Madonna's handprints in concrete

Madonna was the first person to be inducted into the [Wembley Square of Fame](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wembley_Square_of_Fame) in London.

[^cite_note-548]

*[Forbes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forbes)* estimated Madonna's [net worth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Net_worth) at \$850 million as of 2025, making her one of the [wealthiest musicians in the world](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_music_artists_by_net_worth).[^cite_note-FB-549] She became *Forbes*'s annual [highest-paid female musician](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forbes_list_of_the_world's_highest-paid_musicians#Female) 11 times across the 1980s,[^cite_note-550] 1990s,[^cite_note-551] 2000s,[^cite_note-552] and 2010s.[^cite_note-earning2013-553] Madonna is the [best-selling female music artist of all time](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_best-selling_music_artists),[^cite_note-sales-561] and has eighteen albums certified [multi-platinum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Multi-platinum) in multiple countries.[^cite_note-571] According to the [Recording Industry Association of America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Recording_Industry_Association_of_America) (RIAA), she is the best-selling female rock artist of the 20th century and the fourth [highest-certified female artist in the US](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_highest-certified_music_artists_in_the_United_States), with 65.5 million certified [album-equivalent units](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Album-equivalent_units).[^cite_note-572][^cite_note-573]

Madonna has generated over US\$1.6 billion from ticket sales of her concert tours throughout her career.[^cite_note-onlywomantoacheieveboxscore-382] According to *Billboard* Boxscore, she had become the [highest-grossing female touring artist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_highest-grossing_live_music_artists) for 19 years (2004–2023), until being overtaken by [Taylor Swift](5422144.md).[^cite_note-rebelheartgross-318][^cite_note-574] Madonna has won seven [Grammy Awards](44635.md) and twenty-seven [MTV Video Music Awards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MTV_Video_Music_Award), including the 1986 [Video Vanguard Award](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Video_Vanguard_Award) for which she became the first female recipient.[^cite_note-575][^cite_note-576] In Japan, the world's second largest market, Madonna has received seventeen [Gold Disc Awards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Japan_Gold_Disc_Award) from the [Recording Industry Association of Japan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Recording_Industry_Association_of_Japan), including the most [Artist of the Year](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Japan_Gold_Disc_Awards#List_of_the_Artists_of_the_Year) wins by a solo artist (five).[^cite_note-577][^cite_note-578] Madonna became the first artist to be named the "[Greatest Pop Star](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Billboard's_Greatest_Pop_Stars)" of the year twice by *Billboard* (1985 and 1989).[^cite_note-579] *Like a Prayer* (1989), *The Immaculate Collection* (1990), *Ray of Light* (1998), and *Music* (2000) have each been included among *Rolling Stone*'s list titled "[500 Greatest Albums of All Time](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rolling_Stone's_500_Greatest_Albums_of_All_Time)".[^cite_note-b906-580][^cite_note-581]

Throughout her career, Madonna has had twelve songs top the charts in the [United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Artists_with_the_most_number-ones_on_the_Billboard_Hot_100_chart),[^cite_note-hot100-59] thirteen in the [United Kingdom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_artists_with_the_most_UK_singles_chart_number_ones),[^cite_note-582] twenty-four in [Canada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_artists_by_number_of_Canadian_number-one_singles_\(RPM\)),[^cite_note-585] and eleven in [Australia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_artists_who_reached_number_one_on_the_Australian_singles_chart).[^cite_note-586] At the 40th anniversary of the [GfK Media Control Charts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GfK_Entertainment), Madonna was ranked as the most successful singles artist in German chart history.[^cite_note-587] According to *[Billboard](18309966.md)*, she was the most successful solo artist on the *Billboard* Hot 100 by 2021—second overall behind the Beatles—and remains the most successful dance club artist in history.[^cite_note-588][^cite_note-589] She has achieved 38 top-ten singles on the *Billboard* Hot 100, the most by any artist in pre-[streaming](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Music_streaming) era.[^cite_note-590] A dominant [physical singles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Physical_single) seller, she has the most chart-topping hits on the [Hot 100 Singles Sales](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hot_100_Singles_Sales) (16) and the [Dance Singles Sales](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dance_Singles_Sales) (33) of any artist.[^cite_note-591][^cite_note-592] With 50 [Dance Club Songs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dance_Club_Songs) chart-toppers, Madonna became the artist with the most number ones on any single [*Billboard* chart](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Billboard_charts).[^cite_note-DanceRecord-349]

[^cite_note-2]: &#x20;Madonna [goes by her first name](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mononym), and has used the name and trademark since 1979 according to the [World Intellectual Property Organization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Intellectual_Property_Organization).<sup>[\[1\]](#cite_note-1)</sup>

[^cite_note-sales-561]: &#x20;Since 1998, *[Guinness World Records](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guinness_World_Records)* has recognized Madonna as the best-selling female music artist.<sup>[\[553\]](#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGlenday1998228-554)</sup> In 2006, the [International Federation of the Phonographic Industry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Federation_of_the_Phonographic_Industry) (IFPI) officially announced that Madonna had sold over 200 million copies of her albums alone worldwide.<sup>[\[554\]](#cite_note-ifpi.org-555)</sup> Since then, her total [record sales](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Record_sales) have varied from 300 million<sup>[\[555\]](#cite_note-556)</sup><sup>[\[556\]](#cite_note-f464-557)</sup> to 400 million.<sup>[\[557\]](#cite_note-558)</sup><sup>[\[558\]](#cite_note-559)</sup><sup>[\[559\]](#cite_note-560)</sup>

[^cite_note-571]: &#x20;Madonna has 12 albums certified multi-platinum by the RIAA.<sup>[\[560\]](#cite_note-562)</sup> Her other albums certified multi-platinum outside the US are *[Who's That Girl](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Who's_That_Girl_\(soundtrack\))* (1987),<sup>[\[561\]](#cite_note-snep-563)</sup><sup>[\[562\]](#cite_note-564)</sup> *[GHV2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GHV2)* (2001),<sup>[\[563\]](#cite_note-bpi-565)</sup><sup>[\[564\]](#cite_note-566)</sup> *[Confessions on a Dance Floor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Confessions_on_a_Dance_Floor)* (2005),<sup>[\[563\]](#cite_note-bpi-565)</sup><sup>[\[561\]](#cite_note-snep-563)</sup> *[Hard Candy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hard_Candy_\(Madonna_album\))* (2008),<sup>[\[565\]](#cite_note-567)</sup><sup>[\[566\]](#cite_note-568)</sup> *[Celebration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Celebration_\(Madonna_album\))* (2009),<sup>[\[567\]](#cite_note-bracert-569)</sup><sup>[\[563\]](#cite_note-bpi-565)</sup> and *[MDNA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MDNA_\(album\))* (2012).<sup>[\[567\]](#cite_note-bracert-569)</sup><sup>[\[568\]](#cite_note-570)</sup>

[^cite_note-585]: &#x20;Madonna has more number-one singles than any other act in Canadian music history, with 18 singles during the *[RPM](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/RPM_\(magazine\))* era, two singles during the [Canadian Hot 100](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canadian_Hot_100) era, and four singles between 2000 and 2007 on the [Canadian Singles Chart](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canadian_Singles_Chart).<sup>[\[226\]](#cite_note-AllMusicCanada-227)</sup><sup>[\[580\]](#cite_note-583)</sup><sup>[\[581\]](#cite_note-584)</sup>
